**Theorem A′ — the correct $L_\infty\Rightarrow$IBL statement (Task 7, derivation 026 §3.1–§3.4; critics 026A, 026B; part-A fix pass 2026-09-13).** For graded-symmetric $V_n$ on $(\mathcal H,\omega_1)$, the *non-augmented* $\tilde\Delta$-raised family $\{p_{k,\ell}\}_{k,\ell\ge0}$ ($p_{k,0}=V_k$, $p_{0,\ell}=\tilde\Delta^{\otimes\ell}V_\ell$, $p_{k,1}=\ell_k$, $\ell_n=\tilde\Delta\circ V_{n+1}$) satisfies the genus-zero IBL relations in generating-function form at every $(k,\ell)$ iff $(\mathcal H,\omega_1,\ell_n)$ is cyclic $L_\infty$ — it is the polarised master equation $\{S(\Psi+\chi),S(\Psi+\chi)\}=0$ (026 (3.28)–(3.29)), of the non-augmented type CFL admit in §8 (degree bookkeeping needs $d=\tfrac12$, outside CFL's integer-$d$ hypotheses; slot symmetries need the per-slot dictionary 026 §1.2 (iii)). The augmented (CFL Def. 2.3) relations hold only at $\min(k,\ell)=1$: false in general (counterexample at $(2,2)$, $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ CE), at every $(k,\ell)\ge(2,2)$ equivalent to the extra condition $P_{k,\ell}=0$, which fails for some cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra at every such $(k,\ell)$, on the full graded space (026 §3.2, §4, §9 Cor. 9.2). Consequence for the paper: no augmented IBL sub-structure contains $m_2$ for cyclic $L_\infty$ algebras in general; the HS action uses only the $\min(k,\ell)=1$ relations (R11) and is unaffected. Converse: the original Theorem B (braces $L_\infty$ iff $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$) was WRONG (critic 026B: $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ not parity-homogeneous); the corrected statement is Theorem B′ = **R32**.

**Annotation (2026-11-07).** The value $d = \tfrac12$ is forced: among per-strand degrees affine in the ghost number, $3 - N_{\rm gh}$ is the only one reproducing CFL's genus-zero degrees $-2d(m-1)-1$, and it gives $d = \tfrac12$; $N_{\rm gh} - 2$ and every other affine choice give a degree depending on the number of outputs. At $d = \tfrac12$ CFL's genus-counting variable, of degree $2d$, is odd, consistent with their requirement of an integer $d$. Record 114 (2.4), node C3; `codes/verify-ibl-remarks.py` check C3 with break leg, 2026-11-07.

  - Host exclusion for the $\ell\ge2$ components (Verdict (C)): Proven, excl. within the class stated under **Scope**, and the Markl–Voronov no-go on the hosts examined

**Theorem B′ — Koszul braces of a parity-inhomogeneous generator (Task 7 part B re-derived, derivation 026 §5′; critics 026B, 026C; fix pass B′d 2026-09-14).** Let $D=\hat{\mathfrak p}=\sum_{k,\ell}\hbar^{k-1}\hat p_{k,\ell}$ be a finite-order differential operator on a graded-commutative algebra $A$ (components of any parity; $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is parity-inhomogeneous in every grading — parity $\ell$ on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$, $k$ under an odd uniform shift; odd $\hbar$ fixes degrees, not Koszul signs; 026 §5′.1). Its Koszul braces $\ell^W_n=\Phi^n_D$ form a curved $L_\infty$ algebra on $A$ (multilinear relations in all input-parity sectors) iff $F_{D^2}(a)=F_D(a)^2$ for all even $a\in A\otimes\Lambda$, $\Lambda$ any auxiliary Grassmann algebra, $F_D(a):=e^{-a}De^a$ — over even $a\in A$ alone only the even-input relations follow (counterexample $A=\Lambda(\theta)$, $D=\theta\partial_\theta$; 026 W12, critic 026C) — equivalently iff the braces of $D^2$ are the shuffle square of the braces of $D$ (026 (B′.2)), equivalently iff all terms of $e^{-a}D^2e^a$ with at least one derivative of the left factor acting on the output $F_D(a)$ vanish (Lemma W, Wick-type identity $\sum_n\frac1{n!}\Phi^{n+1}_D(a^n,F)=e^{-a}D^2e^a-F^2$, 026 (5′.9)/(W.7)). For odd homogeneous $D$ this is $D^2=0$ (the classical brace theorem, Bering–Damgaard–Alfaro / Akman / Voronov, now with its hypothesis stated); for even or inhomogeneous $D$ it is not: the free theory $\hat{\tilde Q}+\Theta/\hbar$ has curved-$L_\infty$ braces with $D^2\ne0$ (026 §5′.2), and $D=\hat p_{2,0}$ has $(D\star D)_{s\ge1}=0$ with non-$L_\infty$ braces (026 W2). **Refuted:** the earlier Theorem B ("curved $L_\infty$ iff $\hat{\mathfrak p}^2=0$") and the symbol criterion "$(\hat{\mathfrak p}\star\hat{\mathfrak p})_{s\ge1}=0$". On single-string inputs $\ell^W_n(\Psi_1..\Psi_n)=\hbar^{n-1}\sum_\ell p_{n,\ell}$ (Lemma B1) and Theorem A′ (R31) is the leading-$\hbar$ content of the criterion there. Host: theorem host-independent. For $\ell=1$ the naive coderivation extension on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ (026 (5′.12), no décalage sign) is verified to arity 3; for $\ell\ge2$ no single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$ realises the family with A′'s contraction, in the class with slot-uniform parity-homogeneous raising maps on the slots and $\delta$-contraction (026 §5′.7; other hosts — separate input/output generators, non-polynomial product, $\star$-calculus, odd parameter — untested, not excluded); the doubled symbol algebra ($\chi$ in $\bar\varepsilon$, $\Psi$ in $\varepsilon$) is bookkeeping only, not an operator host. HS presentation (b) is *not* the brace structure of any operator (026 (5′.10), W4). **Open (not ledgered):** whether A′ implies the curved $L_\infty$ relations to leading $\hbar$ order on *multi-string* inputs (026 §5′.4, WB2 (b)); the earlier "first obstruction is the one-loop term (5.17)" is withdrawn pending this. **Annotation 2026-09-14, calibrated 2026-09-19 (026 §5′.5–5′.7, WB2 (c)/(b)/(b2); script 142/142, seed 20260908, re-run 2026-09-19 after fix pass 2, which added one check — the graded-commutator step of 026 §8.7.3; critic-reviewed, 026D/026E):** décalage signs — none needed for $\ell=1$: $\hat Q,\hat\ell_2,\hat\ell_3$ close on $S_\varepsilon\mathcal H$ with the naive coderivation extension (5′.12) at arity $\le3$ (026 §5′.5, W6; the $\ell=0$ components untested), so Lemma B1 holds as stated for $\ell=1$ up to arity 3; $S_{\bar\varepsilon}\mathcal H$ admits no sign rule (symmetry type, §1.2 (iii)). The multi-string leading-order corollary is **proven on single strings only (Lemma B1)**; on multi-string inputs it has **no formulation as a Koszul-brace structure of differential operators on a single graded-commutative polynomial host in the strand variables with even $\hbar$** (slot-uniform, parity-homogeneous raising maps, $\delta$-contraction): every such host either projects a slot symmetry (host (i), inconsistent for $\ell\ge2$ — W7″) or replaces A′'s $\tilde\Delta$-contraction by a bare index sum (host (iii), all slots raised — (5′.15), W8b′); MS's odd parameter ($x^2=0$) embeds the $\ell\le2$ slice only. **Untested, not excluded:** hosts with separate input/output generators, a non-polynomial product, the $\star$-calculus (W10), an odd parameter in a non-graded-commutative host. Consequently $\hat{\mathfrak p}$ is **not** an odd square-zero operator on any graded-commutative $\mathbb k[[\hbar]]$-algebra with even $\hbar$ (Markl–Voronov 1511.01591 Def. 1) on the hosts examined — component parity $\ell\bmod2$, $k\bmod2$, or all even. Paper: Theorem B = (B′.4) with the single-string restriction of its $\hbar$-corollary; multi-string extension stated as open *and* as not an operator statement.

**Q13 (030 §6.1) answered "no" within the classes checked. Excl. within Möbius $\mu_0$ discs at sampled $(\beta,r)$, numerical: the (β2) quotient collar is not $\mathcal V_{0,4}$. Correlator-level inequality for any $\mu_0$: argued from the relative-momentum sum, not evaluated (PH9). Ghost anomaly: no obstruction. (Task 13, derivation 031).** (i) The (β2) torus family $\Sigma_{1,4}(s,\theta)$ carries the neck-swapping involution $\iota$ with the four external punctures as fixed points if the rigid slice $\mu_0$ is harmonic ($(1,2;a,b)=(3,4;a',b')=-1$) with $\iota_1$-symmetric discs and the tube is diagonal (Proven); the converse "only if" is argued + numerically supported; the product tube (β1) is excluded for $\lvert s_a-s_b\rvert >C(\mu_0)$ (Proven) and numerically at a generic point. (ii)–(iii) The quotient family is a one-neck $s$-channel collar of an *induced* cubic vertex whose disc at the pair puncture is a limaçon, never SZ's round disc (proven, not by itself decisive); it is not $\mathcal V_{0,4}$ with R2's boundary at any sampled $(\beta,r)$, $\beta\in[0.75,3]$ ($\beta$ free in the $\mathcal V_3'$ family), $r\in[0.3,0.95]$: for $r<r_c(\beta)$ the collar never reaches $\partial F_s$, for $r>r_c$ the necessary tiling condition fails (crossing-$\lambda$ ratio $<1$ throughout; relative mismatch $O(1)$ at $\beta=O(1)$, shrinking in the degenerate large-$\beta$ limit) — numerically supported (Möbius-disc model; checks 3a–4b, 7a–7b); general proof open. (iv) The fixture descends in its soaked part to the $\mathbb Z_2$-orbifold's stubbed untwisted one-neck propagator times $-\tfrac14 b^+_{\rm rel}b^-_{\rm rel}$ (the two unsoaked tube antighosts = zero modes of the $S_2$-odd ghost, antighosts of the frozen $\iota$-odd relative-neck moduli; also the per-strand projection onto $\mathcal H_0\otimes\mathcal H_0$) — Grassmann factorisation Proven (5a–5c); the descent ($L_{\rm tot}=L_0^{\rm orb}$, $b_{\rm tot}=b_0^{\rm orb}$) uses the Lunin–Mathur covering dictionary from memory, unverified. (v) Ghost anomaly: **no obstruction** — all eight torus antighosts stay insertions on the quotient, conservation on the doubled-theory sphere forces $N_{\rm gh}=3$ per twist field, matching the bosonised antiperiodic $\lambda=2$ twist field ($\tfrac32+\tfrac32$); of the two readings 030 §6.1 posed, (A) (total 6) is inadmissible ($3/2\notin\mathbb Z$), (B) (total 12) applies (6a–6e; twist-field weight $h=-9/8$ not reconciled with the Schwarzian $-13/8$, the match does not depend on it). (vi) the 2→2 question: on the quotient the gluing is a 1→1 gluing of two three-punctured spheres of the *doubled* theory; the sphere is the orbifold's, the torus its double cover, the coloured genus 0 the quotient's genus; $\int d^Dk_a$ is the doubled theory's tree-level neck sum. The "no" is correlator-level (loop momentum, 030 §3.7.3: any $\mu_0$ — but 031 l.641 heads that argument "only sketched here" and no correlator was evaluated in either record, so it is argued, not settled: PH9) and geometric ((iii): Möbius-disc model, sampled $(\beta,r)$), not anomalous; the geometric question stays open for non-Möbius (Cassini-type) $\mu_0$ discs, which the loop momentum makes irrelevant to the objective without closing. Not checked: exact tiling and collar overlap (moot); (β1) for every $(q_a,q_b)$; a general proof of (iii) for all $(\beta,r)$; one numerical evaluation of the (β2) composite on four tachyons, which would settle PH9 and the headline.

  - (2) identity (3.2): Proven (024 §3.1) and toy-checked (T1a); $O(g_s^3)$ completion $=$ R17 with mismatch exactly $O(g_s^4)$: Conditional(PH1, PH5) – Numerical (even toy with generic invertible $V_4$; T2, T3, Cauchy-integral Taylor coefficients); "not a consistent truncation": Argued (structural argument, 024 §3.2–§3.3)

**Task 5 — HS action vs the bar–cobar construction (CMZ, arXiv:2608.11307).** (1) Route C (R13) is the cotangent action of the *chain subsystem* of CMZ's canonical factor-2 equations, $B$ undoubled, $\Psi$ kept cyclic; EL solution set modulo the multiplier tower in bijection with their canonical MC set; **not** the cotangent action of their type-I sector as such (the $(2,2)$ split at length 4 and the $L\ge2$ rows have no EL counterpart, they hold identically on the chain); $N_{\rm gh}(\lambda_k)=3k$ conditional on the pairing $\omega_1^{\otimes k}$. (2) $S_{\rm HS}$ at $O(g_s^2)$ = the chain-closed $k\le2$ action (024 (3.1); quartic vertex written quadratically in $\sigma_2$ via the $(2,2)$ split, which Route C drops) with its multiplier replaced by its on-shell value $\lambda_2=-\tfrac{g_s^2}3V_4(\sigma_2,\cdot)$, off-shell identity (3.2); (3.1) differs from Route C at $k\le2$ by exactly that rewriting — the quartic vertex is the pairing the cobar space lacks, degenerate on $\ker V_4$ (R13 quotient); $O(g_s^3)$ completion = R17, mismatch exactly $O(g_s^4)$; **not** a consistent truncation of their equations (exactness of the group-like chain is lost, chain deformed at $O(g_s)$). (3) On the canonical sector their gauge law is R11's composite law plus an EOM term $\mathrm{MC}(\Psi)\wedge\lambda$; canonical-preserving parameters $=\lambda\in\mathcal H_1$ only; their independent $\Lambda_{1,2}$ ($N_{\rm gh}=3$, $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H$) exists, is removed by the gauge $\Phi_{f\ge2}=0$, and is not a symmetry of $S_{\rm HS}$ (R18(1); toy G4 corroborates weakly) — $S_{\rm HS}$ is an action whose equations are equivalent to their canonical-gauge equations order by order in $g_s$ and modulo $\ker V_4$, and whose gauge symmetry is the canonical-gauge residue of theirs; not the gauge-fixed form of their theory, which has no action; whether $S_{\rm HS}$ is the gauge-fixed form of an enlarged action with an independent $\Lambda_1$ stays R18's open Q-can (ST2), unchanged by this analysis. "Canonical-preserving parameters $=\lambda$ only" is CMZ's theorem transcribed, not toy-checked. (4) Their action obstruction 1 (no degree-3 form on the cobar space) is, in the physical grading, $\deg\omega_1^{\otimes\ell}=-\ell-2$ (R7's shift at $\ell=2$); evaded by Route C (duality pairing with a dual tower) and by $S_{\rm HS}$ (even mass form $V_4$, no odd form needed); it **persists** in the BV/odd-symplectic presentation of the type-I tower (R7, 026 §5.5, R18 ST5). Obstruction 2 (cyclicity across factor numbers) is realised on the toy in sharpened form — their undoubled canonical system is EL for *no* constant nondegenerate form — and is void for Route C and $S_{\rm HS}$ (no type-II fields; systems self-adjoint). Scalar $\phi^3+\phi^4$ example (their §7) reproduced by Route C with three multipliers; its HS form collapses to the local Hubbard–Stratonovich field because the contact $V_4$ has a large kernel.

**Reality of the whole auxiliary tower (Task 9a, derivation 028; critic ACCEPT 2026-09-14).** With $\ddagger=(-1)^{N_{\rm gh}+1}\mathrm{bpz}^{-1}\circ\mathrm{hc}$ (conventions C3; Zwiebach's (3.6) on the even-strand fields) extended componentwise to $\mathcal H_0^{\otimes k}$ and the hypothesis (H$_R$) that every geometric $V_n$ is $\ddagger$-equivariant ($V_n(A_i)^*=V_n(A_i^\ddagger)$ — the *conclusion* of Sonoda–Zwiebach H5 plus conjugation invariance of the moduli regions and measures, the same inputs that make the nonpolynomial action real), the tower action of R26 is real at every truncation for $\Sigma_k^\ddagger=\Sigma_k$, $\Psi^\ddagger=\Psi$: all kinetic terms, odd couplings and sources are real, all parents $T_n=-n!\,\mathrm{Pol}(A_{n-2})$ real by induction, and the fixed point preserves reality ($\hat H_k$ real ⇒ $\hat H_k^{-1}$ real, $\ker\hat M_k$ $\ddagger$-stable, on-shell class real). $\Sigma_k^\ddagger=\Sigma_k$ is forced level by level by the sources up to source-kernel directions. Same for any presentation by geometric $V_{a+b+c}(\Sigma_a,\Sigma_b,\Sigma_c)$ with real coefficients (R12). $\chi$: $\chi^\ddagger=\chi$ proven and forced (028 §3; see R24). Route C multipliers: $\lambda_k^\ddagger=\lambda_k$ componentwise; $S_C$ real (028 §4; see R13). **Annotation (2026-09-26).** (Task 9e): the real-polarisation step (028 Step 2.1) is now exercised directly — `codes/verify-reality-tower.py` part 5 reconstructs $T_6=-6!\,\mathrm{Pol}(A)$ from the scalar function $A$ alone (reality, symmetry, exact reconstruction to $4\times10^{-11}$, diagonal identity, $\ddagger$-equivariance at complex arguments; break legs: a complex coefficient, a wrong $(n-1)!$); 55/55 PASS, re-run by the orchestrator. (i) (H$_R$), $V_n(A_i)^*=V_n(A_i^\ddagger)$, is stated for all arguments; as written it holds for Grassmann-even arguments only: on general arguments the correct form is $V_n(A_i^{\ddagger'})=(-1)^{k_o(k_o-1)/2}V_n(A_i)^*$, $k_o$ the number of Grassmann-odd arguments, i.e. H1 of record 075 (R81) with the Koszul sign (counter-example to the unsigned form: $V_2(A,B)=\langle A|c_0^-Q_B|B\rangle$ on ghost numbers $(1,3)$, sign $-1$); the tower (every strand of $\Sigma_k$, $\Psi$, $\chi$, $\sigma_n$) uses even arguments only, and the odd-$k$ multipliers rest on the equivariance of the pairing, not of a vertex on odd arguments, so (H$_R$) is meant in this entry on the even sector, where nothing changes. (ii) Record 028 (C1) writes $\ddagger=\mathrm{BPZ}\circ\mathrm{hc}$; every statement of the entry holds for $\ddagger'=(-1)^{N_{\rm gh}+1}\mathrm{bpz}^{-1}\circ\mathrm{hc}$ (the entry's body and Scope already say so, conventions C3), the bare $\mathrm{bpz}^{-1}\circ\mathrm{hc}$ giving $\Psi^\star=-\Psi$ and an anti-equivariant pairing. - **Status**:
  - Morphism failure ($\mathrm{Sym}^2$ two-input identity fails at $O(g^0)$ by $\hat Q(\Psi^{\otimes2})$), $\mathcal H_0$-component exact, defect $=-dF[\mathcal L(\Psi)]\rvert_{\mathrm{Sym}^2}$ (on-shell morphism): Proven (one-line polynomial identities (2.1)–(2.7) given (1.5), 029 §2); (1.5) and the boxed identities checked numerically against the action, 16/16, two seeds
  - Not repaired by any normalisation of $\phi_2$: Proven (029 §0 "Components needed")
  - No repair by a $\phi_3$: Argued (generic-data argument, $\phi_3$ untested)
  - Grading on and off the classical complex: Argued, supported by check G

**The comparison map $(\phi_1,\phi_2)=(\iota,\cdot^{\otimes2})$ is NOT an $L_\infty$-morphism; on-shell morphism only (Task 9b = T5, derivation 029).** $F:\mathsf T\to\mathsf E$ from the quartic-truncated cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra $(\mathcal H_0;Q,\ell_2,\ell_3)$ to presentation (b) on $\mathcal H_0\oplus\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0$ (brackets read off the HS action) satisfies the $\mathcal H_0$-component of the morphism identity exactly to all orders and the $\mathrm{Sym}^2$-component only on shell; the two-input identity fails at $O(g^0)$ by $\hat Q(\Psi^{\otimes2})$ ($\Sigma$ has no $Q$-kinetic term) — the operator mismatch $\hat Q$ vs $m_2$, convention-independent; not repaired by any normalisation of $\phi_2$ (proof); repair by a $\phi_3$ excluded for generic $(Q,\ell_3)$ by a generic-data argument only ($\phi_3$ untested); the defect equals $-dF[\mathcal L(\Psi)]\rvert_{\mathrm{Sym}^2}$, so $F$ maps MC elements to MC elements (R8) and its gauge component is R11 — that is its full content; the projection direction is blocked by the same term and needs $m_2^{-1}$ (I3). **Paper: state the equivalence as elimination of an auxiliary field (R8/R10, R11, R22). The morphism failure is for $\phi_1=\iota$ fixed; a quasi-isomorphism by homological-perturbation transfer along $m_2^{-1}$ is PH8 (with PH1), not attempted in 029 and supported since by R41 (finite-dimensional model, on the classical complex, Conditional(PH1)).** Grading (029 §1 (ii), check G): $\tilde\ell_1=Q\oplus(-\tfrac{g^2}{12}m_2)$ is not parity-homogeneous in $N_{\rm gh}\bmod2$; with $\deg_{\mathcal H_0}=N_{\rm gh}-2$, $\deg_{\mathrm{Sym}^2}=(N_{\rm gh}-4)/2$ every bracket restricted to the classical complex (gh $\{2,3\}\oplus\{4,6\}$) has degree $+1$ and $\omega_2$ degree $-1$ (the $L_\infty$ structure of 026 §5.4 is $\mathbb Z$-graded cyclic there); this grading is half-integer on odd-ghost $\mathrm{Sym}^2$ states (gauge directions $\Lambda\otimes\Psi$), and no grading that is a function of total ghost number makes the brackets homogeneous on the full space (slope and offset constraints incompatible, surviving mod 2). Also $\tilde\ell_1^2=O(g^4)\ne0$ on the full $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0$, consistent with R11's "to $O(g^3)$". Not checked: odd-input sectors, the $\phi_3$-exact escape, uniqueness, CFT $V_4$. **Annotation 2026-09-21 (R41):** the transfer was attempted (037): it reproduces $\mathsf T$ exactly and gives a quasi-isomorphism on the classical complex, in the finite-dimensional model and conditionally on PH1; R36's failure is the same map read off that complex, at $N_{\rm gh}=5$. Paper directive: no *unqualified* quasi-isomorphism claim; a qualified one must carry the classical-complex restriction, PH1, and the fact that the map is not strict.

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/032-v3prime-route-feasibility.md` §0 (verdict table), §2 (precondition (P)), §3 (covering map), §4 (operator identity (6.2), §4.4 verdict (ii)), §5 (seam question, 14d), §6 (verdict (iii)), §7 (assumptions A1–A7)

- **Status**:
  - (P) $r_{\max}$, $\sigma_{\max}$: Proven (closed form (2.4), 032 §2), checked numerically (P1a–f); cut convention stated, not chosen
  - (i) covering with the $S_3$ seam, never with constant $\sigma$: Numerical (Euclidean area, grid $h=0.004$, $\beta=3/2$; seam under-determined)
  - (ii) operator identity (6.2) on shell: Conditional(PH10) – Argued: it reduces to the covering (6.1), checked numerically (rel. diff $\sim10^{-3}$ at two generic momenta, C3a–c), and the measure identity (4.5), argued by hand and not machine-checked
  - (iii) seam geometry: first-order agreement on the $S_3$ seam and the $12:1$ count: Proven (reflection (5.4)), checked C4c, C4g; second-order disc mismatch $\sim15\,\%$ on every admissible seam: Numerical (exact Möbius germs, C4k, C4d3 ray scan; $\alpha'=0$, $\beta'=3/2$, $\gamma'=9.5$)
  - (iii) off-shell failure on non-primary externals: Argued (not evaluated); collar term (5.2) a sufficient cure: Conditional(PH11) – Argued; necessity not shown

**Single-strand $\mathcal V_3'$ route (α-rev), bounded feasibility study (Task 14, derivation 032).** (P) The special-leg disc of $\mathcal V_3'$ extends to radius $r$ and stays embedded iff $r\le r_{\max}=\frac{2\beta'(\gamma'-1)}{\gamma'+1}$ ($\alpha'=0$); $\sigma_{\max}=\ln r_{\max}$ for a one-sided plumbing cut, $2\ln r_{\max}$ for the symmetric cut; $=0$ for the tangent (SZ-type) vertex — computed, self-checked. (i) The reversed-range ($\lvert q\rvert >1$) family covers the interior $\mathcal M'_\times$ once for the $S_3$-symmetric seam $\sigma(\theta)\in[0.434,0.880]$ ($\pi$-periodic; needs $\gamma'\ge3.15$ symmetric / $9.19$ one-sided, $\Phi\in\mathcal H^{\rm even}$, twist projector $\int d\theta\,e^{i\theta L_0^-}(e^{\sigma(\theta)L_0^+}-1)/L_0^+$), **never for constant $\sigma$** (gap closes at $\sigma^*=0.879$, overlap starts at $0.434$) — computed (Euclidean area, grid 0.004, $\beta=3/2$; seam under-determined). (ii) The operator identity 030 (6.2) holds on shell to the covering accuracy (rel. diff $\sim10^{-3}$) under the sewing-convergence hypothesis PH10 (sewing converges on $\lvert q\rvert>1$ up to the $\sigma_{\max}$ of (P)); the sewing measure on $\lvert q\rvert >1$ is the pull-back of the canonical measure (Jacobian $\lvert q\rvert ^2\lvert Z'_\alpha\rvert ^2$) — argued (measure) + computed; (6.2) adds no constraint beyond the covering. (iii) Across the $S_3$ seam the transported coordinates agree at first order up to a common phase (reflection $F^{(t)}_i=\mathsf R\circ F^{(s)}_{\pi(i)}\circ{\rm conj}$, $\pi=(23)$; 4 real conditions met) and differ at second order by $\sim15\,\%$ of the disc radius on every admissible seam ($12:1$ count; at $\alpha'=0$ $\kappa_1^{(s)}=-1$, $\kappa_1^{(t)}=0$: the $t$-disc at puncture 1 is centred, the $s$-disc off-centre) — proven + computed; hence the four-input $L_\infty$ relation is expected to fail off shell **on non-primary external states** by the BRST seam term (5.1′) (the seam term vanishes identically on total-primary externals in $\mathcal H_0$, on or off shell) — argued, not evaluated; the collar quartic term (5.2) is a sufficient cure under a stated, unchecked embedding condition (PH11); necessity not shown (a modified neck map is an open cure). **Verdict: conditional go with a collar quartic term; strictly cubic version conjectured, not shown, to fail off shell.** Question 1: a sharp seam with coincident coordinates exists at first order (the $S_3$ seam) but not at second order; question 2: yes, the $S_3$ seam covers the interior once, a constant bound cannot. Decisive number not computed: (5.1′) on a level-one non-primary at generic momentum, summed over the three seams.

  - Deformed-shift closure (1.2′), the common-$\rho$ structural case, the list of dependent rows: Argued (not machine-checked)

**(5.1′) — compatibility of the $\ker V_4$ quotient with $V_5$ (plan Task 15 Q1, derivation 034; critics 2026-09-19: iteration 1 REVISE 0F/3S, iteration 2 ACCEPT).** The quotient is compatible with the $O(g_s^3)$ coupling iff $V_5(\ker V_4,\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0,\mathcal H_0)=0$ (matrix test $\Pi_NV_{5S}[:,:,a]=0$, $\Pi_N=1-V_{4S}V_{4S}^+$); for totally symmetric $V_5$ also necessary and sufficient for $O(g_s^3)$ matching (off-shell in $\Psi$) in every complement (polarisation); without it the 5-point tree is contaminated by $-\tfrac{g_s^3}{5!}[V_5(P,P,\Psi)-V_5(\pi_CP,\pi_CP,\Psi)]$ (complement-dependent), and keeping kernel modes with the dressed kernel gives a rational degree-5 term $-\tfrac{g_s^3}{5!}v_N^\top W_{NN}^{-1}v_N$, $O(g_s^{-3})$ kernel propagator, no vacuum propagator ($O(g_s^2)$ contamination if $W_{NN}$ singular); holds automatically when all $\Sigma$-couplings factor through a common linear map $\rho$ with $V_4$ non-degenerate on $\operatorname{im}\rho$ (R34 scalar toy); remedy: multiplier pair on $\ker V_4$ (hybrid Route C), cubic, closes at $O(g_s^3)$; $\ker V_4=0$ reproduces R17; R17–R22, R24–R26, R30, R33, R34(2) assume the condition or $\ker V_4=0$ (see PH5).

**Annotation (2026-09-19, R38).** The (5.1) defusal above holds at $O(g_s^2)$ only. From $O(g_s^3)$ the quotient $\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0/\ker V_4$ is consistent iff $V_5(\ker V_4,\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0,\mathcal H_0)=0$; R17–R22, R24–R26, R30, R33 and R34(2) assume this condition or $\ker V_4=0$ (R15 only where $V_4^{-1}$ enters $V_5$-composites). Whether $\ker V_4\neq0$ for the actual vertices is the numerical test of plan Task 15 Q2 (pending vertex data). Source: derivation 034.

**R38 annotation (2026-10-01, R75).** Remedy (a) continues at $O(g_s^4)$: with the dressed vertex $W_6^{(C)}=V_6-\tfrac65\mathcal C\vert_{V_4^{-1}\to G_C}$ the hybrid matches $S^{(6)}_{\rm eff}$ in every complement; the $O(g_s^4)$ feedback of §3(a) item (i), $\tfrac{g_s^4}{600}\langle v,G_Cv\rangle$, is quadratic in the complement parameter $L$ and constant iff $(C_{\rm ver})$ holds. The "expected, not verified" of item (i) is discharged; the (1.2) condition remains that of the *quotient* presentation.

### R39 — Negative-moduli calculus; necessary conditions for $\mathcal V_5p_{2,2}^{-1}\mathcal V_5$ to be a chain of $\mathcal M_{0,6}$ [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/035-negative-moduli-composite.md` claim tree (A1–A5, C1–C4), §1 (calculus, inverse lemma (1.6)), §2 (Prop. 2.1, conditions (i)–(v), (2.4′)), §3 (toy), §4.1–§4.3 (verdict, hand-off, not checked)
- **Scope**: fixed level truncation (035 A3); genus 0; the level-truncation limit of $Z_L$ not controlled (PH19), and the fixed-level object $V_5^{(L)\top}(V_4^{(L)})^{-1}V_5^{(L)}$ is not the truncation of the composite; (iv) is an exclusion within chains with vertex-independent or polynomial coefficients, uniform on the family $(tV_4,uV_5)$ — coefficients tuned at the actual data are outside the class and open; internal momentum integral and multiple-zero isotropy in the real vertices not checked
- **Origin**: the `Argued` parts – `agent/derivations/035-negative-moduli-composite.md`, as carried by `agent/reports/general-purpose-ledger-E-20260926T133000.md`
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-21 – exact check – `codes/verify-negative-moduli.py` part 1: $\hat d=5\ell-6-\gamma$ transcription-checked on all 17 rows of 030 §1.3; parts 2–3 (C2.3 group count, C2.4 degree, toy residues), 20 checks PASS (16a)
  - 2026-09-21 – literature comparison – Kimura–Stasheff–Voronov (hep-th/9307114), review `literature/reviews/3EQKEUBW-*`: $\dim_\R\overline{\mathcal M}_n=2n-6$ for $n\ge3$, stability required, no $n=2$ case

- **Status**:
  - (1) calculus ($\hat d$, $\hat d_B$, additivity, inverse lemma, closure corollaries): Proven (finite bookkeeping), transcription-checked; unstable values by R59
  - (2)(i) isotropy / R38 condition at every zero of $\det V_4(P^2)$ off the mass shells: Conditional(PH17) – Proven at fixed level, modulo the standard factorisation/sewing theorem (035's A2 = 022 §2.5, cited)
  - (2)(iii) one $G$-invariant chain per $(3\vert3)$ splitting, $\lvert G\rvert=72$: Proven (035 (2.6)), checked numerically (C2.3, stabiliser of order 72 on random data, break leg)

**Task 16a: negative-moduli calculus and necessary conditions for $\mathcal V_5p_{2,2}^{-1}\mathcal V_5$ to be a chain of $\mathcal M_{0,6}$ (derivation 035).** (1) Calculus: $\hat d=S-6$ (forms), $\hat d_B=6-S_B$ (bivectors), additive under gluing, $=\dim_\R\mathcal M_{0,n}$ on integrated vertices with handles at $-6$; inverse lemma $\hat d_B(\Omega^{-1})=-\hat d(\Omega)$ (proven: the inverse of a homogeneous form, invertible on the graded space or on its graded quotient by the kernel, has the same ghost support); affine to the map ghost number, $\hat d=5\ell-6-\gamma$, transcription-checked on all 17 rows of 030 §1.3; closure $2-2+2$, $2-2+4-2+2$, $4-2+4$ as corollaries. Standard only in part: $\dim_\R\overline{\mathcal M}_n=2n-6$ for $n\ge3$ (Kimura–Stasheff–Voronov, hep-th/9307114, who require stability and state no $n=2$ case or cylinder — checked against the paper, review `literature/reviews/3EQKEUBW-*`), the continuation to the unstable cases being taken here (derived since in R59); Zwiebach 1992, hep-th/9206084 for the $b$-ghost forms and the BRST–boundary identity); the $-6g$ handle term, the bivector reading and the inverse lemma are this record's. Necessary, never sufficient (double tube and $p_{2,2}^{-1}$ share $\hat d_B=-2$; negative $\hat d$ is not by itself an obstruction — the discussion's "no family has negative dimension" holds for $n_b$, not $\hat d$). (2) Necessary conditions for $\mathcal C=\sum c_\alpha\int_{\Gamma_\alpha}$ with $\Gamma_\alpha,c_\alpha$ **independent of the external momenta** (A5, carried as a declared hypothesis, PH17: the hypothesis that makes (H) a worldsheet representation; without it a coefficient of degree $-1$ in $V_4$ carries the pole and (i) says nothing): (i) every zero $P_0$ of $\det V_4(P^2)$ at fixed level lies on a mass shell, or the image $W(P_0)=\{N^\top V_5(P_0)(\cdot,A,z)\}$ is totally isotropic for $(N^\top\partial V_4N)^{-1}$ — at a simple zero, the R38 condition $\Pi_{N(P_0)}V_5(P_0)[:,:,a]=0$ at the complex point $P_0$ (Prop. 2.1: chain integrals with standard node insertions are singular only on mass shells, entire if bounded — closes the unbounded-chain and boundary-measure loopholes modulo the factorisation input, except for coincidence; off the real axis the conclusion survives even non-standard approach to the divisor); (v) residue factorisation at coincidence points; (ii′) BRST closure, recursive; (iii) one $G$-invariant chain per $(3\vert3)$ splitting with $G=(S_3\times S_3)\rtimes\mathbb Z_2$, $\vert G\vert =72$, $10=720/72$ images under $S_6$, R19's 90 labelled assignments being the Wick count $10\times9$; (iv) degree $(-1,2)$: `excl. within chains with vertex-independent or polynomial coefficients, uniform on the family $(tV_4,uV_5)$` (R28′ transplanted); coefficients tuned at the actual data are outside the class and remain open; silent at the actual data. (3) Toy (numpy, $\dim N=1,2$, 24/24 PASS, contour residue vs Laurent formula $v_0^\top N(N^\top M_1N)^{-1}N^\top v_0$, two break-on-purpose legs): the pole survives on generic $V_5$, is removed iff $W$ is isotropic, reappears $\propto\varepsilon^2$ when a generic null direction is sourced; at $\dim N=2$ an isotropic $W$ gives zero residue with $\Pi_NV_5\ne0$, so R38 is sufficient but not necessary there — (i) is not vacuous and not implied by R38. **Verdict: not excluded at the actual data; excluded as a uniform construction; first falsifier (under PH17) = complex zero locus of $\det V_4^{(L)}(P^2)$ with the isotropy/R38 test on it, at fixed level — decisive for (H) only together with the $L\to\infty$ behaviour of $Z_L$, which is open (hand-off 16b / Task 15 Q2; cheapest indicator, a model and not a truncation: shared complex zero of $V_4^{TT}$ and $V_5^{TTTTT}$ at frozen relative momentum).** Not checked: the level-truncation limit of $Z$ — sharper, as 035 l.321 states it: the fixed-level object $V_5^{(L)\top}(V_4^{(L)})^{-1}V_5^{(L)}$ is *not* the truncation of the composite, which is what stops the 16b outcome from being read as a verdict on (H) (transfer registered as PH19); internal momentum integral; genus $\ge1$; (ii′)/(v); momentum-independence of the chains (A5, now a declared hypothesis, PH17, not an assumption); multiple-zero isotropy in the real vertices. The continuation of $\dim_\R\overline{\mathcal M}_n=2n-6$ to the unstable cases, taken here from no source, was PH20; it is derived in R59 (PH20 discharged 2026-09-26). **Re-scoped 2026-09-21 (rigor audit):** (iv) was given the class 035 l.320 proves it on; A5 and the two transfer assumptions became declared hypotheses (PH17, PH19, PH20) instead of parenthetical "assumed"; the level-truncation item was restored to the source's sharper wording. *Annotation 2026-09-26:* PH20 (the unstable values of the calculus) discharged by R59 — no longer a hypothesis of this row.

**Annotation (2026-09-21, R39).** The dressed sextic vertex $W_6=V_6-\tfrac65\mathcal C$ of R19 is excluded as a worldsheet construction *uniform in the vertex data* (the R28′ degree argument, transplanted to the composite in 035 §2 (iv)), but is **not** excluded at the actual vertices: R39 gives the necessary conditions and a first fixed-level falsifier. R38's condition reappears there in a new role — imposed at the *complex* zeros of $\det V_4(P^2)$ rather than on the physical kernel, and in the weaker isotropy form (2.4′) when the zero is multiple. R28/R28′ are untouched: the theorem is about $p_{2,2}^{-1}$ alone, and R39 is about the composite.

**Task 17: what the $\Sigma$-composite covers in $\mathcal M_{0,6}$ (derivation 036).** (1) CKV reading of R39's calculus: at genus 0, $\hat d=n_b-n_c$ = (integrated moduli) − (residual CKV directions), the Riemann–Roch virtual dimension, $-6=\dim_\R\mathrm{PSL}(2,\C)$; the example made exact — the plumbing parameter $q=e^{-s+i\theta}$ is a point of the residual $\C^*$ of the two-punctured sphere, inversion turns the two $c$'s of $Q_B$ into the $b_0^\pm$ of $\mathsf P$. $\hat d(\mathcal V_{2k}(\Sigma_k,\Sigma_k))=4k-6$, negative only for $k=1$, where the reading is $(n_b,n_c)=(0,2)$ and not $(4k-6,0)$; removal law $R(k)=4k-6=\dim\mathcal M_{0,2k}$ for every $(a,b)$ (an algebraic identity given R39's additivity and inverse lemma, transcription-checked; the unstable value it uses at $k=1$ is R59's). (2) A geometric $s$-strand sewing object obeys $S_B=6+6g-n_b+n_c$ with $g=\sum g_i+2s-C-1$ read off the **connectivity** (two cylinders $\Rightarrow g=1$, one connected four-punctured sphere $\Rightarrow g=2$), and $n_b\le n_b^{\max}=6g+6-4s$ (two independent counts); hence the floor $S_B\ge4s+n_c$. $p_{2,2}^{-1}$ ($S_B=8$, $s=2$) sits exactly on it: $n_c=0$ and $n_b$ saturated at **every** connectivity and every component genus — no $c$-insertion pattern reaches $p_{2,2}^{-1}$, and $\hat d_B=-2$ is $6-4s$, not a CKV surplus (the "four moduli minus one handle" reading is the $g=1$ branch only). **No uniqueness**: the capless solutions are the disconnected double tube ($g=1$, $n_b=4$), excluded by strand factorisation (030 Prop. 3.1–3.2), and a connected four-punctured sewing sphere ($g=2$, $n_b=10$: its two Beltrami antighosts and a full plumbing fixture $b_0^+b_0^-\delta_{L_0^-,0}/L_0^+$ on each of its four legs; the count is that of the sewing bivector on $\mathcal H$ – 030 §1.3 row 14 counts the same object as $n_b=6$, $n_x=4$, because it books the four $b_0^-\delta_{L_0^-,0}$ to the $\omega_1$ attachments), which that exclusion does not reach. **Neither is barred by R28**: the double tube is disconnected, and the connected sphere carries eight antighosts beyond the Beltrami measure of its own surface (trivial Beltrami differentials on external legs), which 022's item (3) does not admit – it is 030 §2.2 row (d), outside R28's class, excluded by R28′ as a uniform construction only and open at the actual $V_4$ (R28 scope line; 030 §3.3). Sewn into $\mathcal V_4\,p_{2,2}^{-1}\,\mathcal V_4$ it is a genus-two surface with the full fourteen-dimensional antighost measure of $\mathcal M_{2,4}$, the two-loop analogue of (β)'s four-punctured tori (R30). The enumeration is not subsumed by R28. *Erratum 2026-10-04, corrected 2026-10-10 (node check):* the double tube is excluded by strand factorisation (030 Prop. 3.1–3.2); the connected sphere is outside R28's class and excluded only as a uniform construction (R28′); the exclusion of both as uniform constructions stands, the connected one is open at the actual $V_4$. Capped patterns are excluded by rank. Counterfactually — the colour-2 edge carries no modulus (R15, R27 trap T3) — a geometric composite would be a top form of dimension $\dim_\R\mathcal M_{g,6}$ ($12$ at $g=1$, $18$ at $g=2$), sphere-equivalent $6$. (3) Collapse criterion, **as an iff** (Proposition 2.6): with the vertex forms of $S_4$ ($\mathcal V_5$, $W_6$) as the data, every $\mathrm{Sym}^2$ slot of $S_4$ through $O(g_s^4)$ is affine in $p_{2,2}$ — interaction vertices $V$-independent, the source $p_{2,2}\Psi^2$ linear — and a $\Sigma$ line $u^\top V^{-1}w$ is a polynomial in the data iff one end is a pure source; proof by the degree count $\deg(u_0^\top\mathrm{adj}V\,w_0)=n-1<n=\deg\det V$ and density of symmetric matrices (no irreducibility needed). Identity in *independent* data, like the four- and five-point collapses (it excludes a universal algebraic collapse, not one forced by the $L_\infty$ relations at the actual vertices); R19 (2) is its six-point instance. The five-point double collapse requires R38's condition ($\mathcal V_5$ vanishing on $\ker p_{2,2}$ — falsifiable check). Hence $W_4=0$, $W_5=\mathcal V_5$, $W_6\ne\mathcal V_6$ as one statement. At the actual level-$L$ data: $\mathcal C^{(L)}$ is meromorphic with a pole at every non-isotropic zero of $\det V_4^{(L)}(P^2)$, a vertex is entire — non-collapse under H2a. (4) Covering: $\mathcal M_{0,6}=\mathcal F_6\sqcup\mathcal V_{0,6}$ is A2 (Zwiebach, imported), with $\mathcal V_{0,6}$ covered by $W_6+\tfrac65\mathcal C$ identically — a definition plus A2, so the content is (3) and the classification below. Hypothesis **H2a** (036): $\mathcal C^{(L)}(P)$ has a pole off the plumbing lattice $P_I^2=-\tfrac2{\alpha'}(N+\bar N-2)$ — a non-isotropic zero of $\det V_4^{(L)}(P^2)$ there; open, decided by 16b in either direction (H1 does not imply it; H2b, $\mathcal C^{(L)}$ entire, is the exclusive alternative under which only the Feynman-region exclusion survives). Under H2a (its off-shell part) or H2b, $\mathcal C$ is vertex-type and not a union of Feynman regions (which have mass-shell poles with non-vanishing residues); if every pole is on the physical mass shells the fallback discriminator is the residue rank (one at a simple zero of $\det V_4$, against the level degeneracy). Of the three ways the $\mathcal V_{0,6}$ split could be realised: (i) `excl. within sub-regions with momentum-independent boundary and the standard measure (the qualifier carrying A2), under H2a's existence part` — excluded anywhere in $\mathcal M_{0,6}$, given a pole of $\mathcal C^{(L)}$ in $P$ (H2a = PH18, existence part; compactness; the "standard measure" qualifier carries 035's A2 content, and the momentum-independence it presupposes is PH17); (ii) a momentum-dependent region with the standard measure is `excl. within class (a)–(c) of §3.5 (bounded multiplicity, real-analytic in $P$, collar floor $\epsilon\ge\epsilon_{\min}>0$), under H2a (PH18)` for **sector-type ends** at the degeneration divisors (Proposition 3.5: bounded multiplicity, real-analytic in $P$, $r<\epsilon(\theta,m',P)$ with $\epsilon\ge\epsilon_{\min}>0$ — meromorphic with poles on the plumbing lattice only, the physical mass shells for full angular support, the full lattice of spacing $2/\alpha'$ for partial support; under H2a) — and, outside that class, **constructed**: the third member of §3.2's trichotomy **survives** for cusp-type ends, $r<c(\theta-\theta_0)^k$ gives a simple pole at $P_I^2=-\mu_\phi^2-2/(\alpha'k)$, off the lattice iff $1/k\notin\mathbb Z$ ($k=2$, not $k=1$), any prescribed position below the channel threshold, $P_0^2<-\mu_\phi^2$ ($k>0$), for analytic $k(P)$ (eq. 3.6, quadrature-checked with residue); other $\epsilon\to0$ profiles give non-pole singularities; equality with $\mathcal C^{(L)}$ — every zero of $\det V_4^{(L)}$, residues with their rank, analyticity elsewhere — is a named open computation (residue matching on 16b's zero locus), registered as PH22; (iii) the reading adopted is a momentum-weighted density, of which the cusp region ($\{0,1\}$-valued weight on the sector, by definition of an indicator) is a drawable representative; nothing places $\mathcal C$ in $\mathcal V_{0,6}$ but the definition of $W_6$. R33 argues, at correlator level and for any $\mu_0$, that the (β) fixture is not a representation of the $V_4$ theory (conditional on PH9, argued and not evaluated; loophole: non-Möbius discs) and records that the ghost anomaly is not the obstruction. Live geometric options: R30's (α-rev)/Task 14, untouched by (2); the open cases of R28′, open at the actual $V_4$ only (explicit-ghost dressings, mixed tube/slice kernels, a dressing tuned at the actual $V_4$); and the cusp region of (3.6) — the one constructive geometric object of the record, a momentum-dependent region touching a divisor in a cusp $r<c(\theta-\theta_0)^k$ that carries a simple pole at any prescribed position below the channel threshold, $P_0^2<-\mu_\phi^2$ ($k>0$), for analytic $k(P)$, held back by the single named computation PH22 — a drawable representative of the density class, the region/density distinction being definitional (036 §3.6). (5) Scalar control: $\phi^3{+}\phi^4{+}\phi^5{+}\phi^6$ with HS $\sigma$ gives feedback $\lambda_5^2\phi^6/(600\lambda_4)$ and $\lambda_6'=\lambda_6-\tfrac65\lambda_5^2/\lambda_4$ (R17's $1/600$ and R19's $6/5$ reproduced by an independent route; sympy, with a break leg); collapse criterion, coefficients and covering identity survive there, while everything geometric, the $\mathcal F_6$ term and the kernel/quotient (R38) structure have no counterpart. ### R41 — Finite-dim. model: integrating $\Sigma$ out is an HPT quasi-isomorphism on the classical complex [Conditional(PH1) – Verified (finite-dimensional model)]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/037-hpt-transfer-sigma.md` §1 (classical complex, contraction data), §2 (transferred brackets (2.4), (2.7), (2.9), transfer map), §3 (morphism identities, quasi-isomorphism), §4 (obstruction off the classical complex), §5
- **Scope**: finite-dimensional model of 029 §3 on the classical complex (degrees $\{0,1\}$), generic random tensors, even inputs only; the infinite-dimensional statement is PH1 (the model exhibits only the injectivity half, R9); controls the equations of motion and their linearisation, not the BRST spectrum nor the gauge algebra; excl. within extensions by gauge parameters ($N_{\rm gh}=1$) or antifields ($N_{\rm gh}=4$), gradings by total ghost number, brackets read off $S_{\rm HS}$ — outside: a presentation giving $\Sigma$ a $Q$-kinetic term (R30), gradings not functions of total ghost number, $\phi_2$ of slope $\ne1$; weakest relaxation: an invertible $\mathcal B$ of $N_{\rm gh}=-1$ with $m_2=\mathcal B\mathfrak m$, existence open (037 C5.4)
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-21 – numerical check – `codes/verify-hpt-transfer.py` (first pass, 037 §1–§5), transfer run as a numerical fixed-point recursion, brackets by Vandermonde fit – superseded by the fix-pass run (next line)
  - 2026-09-21 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-037-20260921T164500.md` – REVISE ("the row must not be promoted as written": A2 repair, R7 identification); fix pass: 30 named checks, 30/30 PASS on seeds 2037, 7, 11, break legs C1-rank′ … C5.1′
  - 2026-09-21 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-037-iter2-20260921T190000.md` – REVISE light (bijectivity/non-degeneracy iff restricted to the model; "linearised spectrum" replaced); promotion allowed as `Conditional(PH1) – Verified (finite-dimensional model)`; light fix pass, script re-run by the orchestrator: 31/31 report lines PASS on seeds 2037, 7, 11 (30 distinct checks; 037)

**Integrating $\Sigma$ out as a homological-perturbation transfer (derivation 037).** Finite-dimensional model of 029 §3, classical complex (fields and equations of motion, degrees $\{0,1\}$), $\Sigma$ sector $=\mathrm{Sym}^2$ of the truncated $\mathcal H_0$ with the ghost-block projector (the restricted variational problem of the HS action). The projected differential $m_2^{\rm cl}=\Pi_2m_2$ is the form $V_4$ on $S^{(4)}$: injectivity $\iff$ non-degeneracy of $V_4$ there (R9's half of PH1); bijectivity in the model; beyond it the contraction needs PH1's level-uniform half as restated 2026-09-22 — the inverse on the quotient existing level by level with a stable kernel, so that $h$ has a level limit (R10) — not a bounded inverse; a contraction of *topological* complexes would need more and is not claimed. Result: the transferred brackets are exactly $(Q,g\ell_2,g^2\ell_3,0)$ – an explicit $\Sigma$-exchange tree, the second route R10 lacked; the transfer map is $(\iota,\cdot^{\otimes2})$ with $\phi_{n\ge3}=0$ and the normalisation $c=1$ derived; it is a cyclic $L_\infty$ quasi-isomorphism, not strict. Scope: on a two-term complex the $L_\infty$ relations are vacuous beyond those listed, so the content is (i) the brackets, (ii) $\iota_*$ an isomorphism on cohomology (PH1 restated), (iii) cyclicity; it controls the equations of motion and their linearisation, not the BRST spectrum nor the gauge algebra (R11 remains the gauge statement; the gauge algebra at $O(g_s^3)$, $O(g_s^4)$ is R44, R45, R57, the BRST operator around a background R73, R76). excl. within extensions by gauge parameters ($N_{\rm gh}=1$) or antifields ($N_{\rm gh}=4$), gradings by total ghost number, brackets read off $S_{\rm HS}$: no extension of the quasi-isomorphism ($N_{\rm gh}(m_2)=+2$ where $+1$ is needed); outside the class: a presentation giving $\Sigma$ a $Q$-kinetic term (R30); weakest relaxation: a factorisation $m_2=\mathcal B\mathfrak m$ with $N_{\rm gh}(\mathcal B)=-1$, existence open.

### R42 — $\chi$ scheme with elementary vertices: exactly enough freedom to close at $O(g_s^5)$ (paper count) [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/038-chi-scheme-ogs5-count.md` §1–§5 (counting rules, order formula, strand-locality lemma, $O(g_s^5)$ menu, closure count), §6 (outcome, (E4), $c_6$ pinned); `agent/derivations/048-chi-scheme-ogs5-matching.md` (matching appendix, §8 C6)
- **Scope**: field content $(\Psi,\Sigma_2,\chi,\Sigma_3)$, bare geometric (elementary) vertices only; order by order ($O(g_s^5)$, nothing about $O(g_s^6)$); presentations with $b'_\chi=0$ (condition (E4)) — the $b'_\chi\neq0$ branch run only as a break leg (048 C6: out-of-span component at $O(g_s^5)$, structure open; R24's $O(g_s^4)$ closure fails there, 048 (8.2)); the sextic split pinned by convention at $c_6=0$, fitted values quoted there; the matching appendix in a random-tensor model on three seeds, C6 open
- **Verification**:

- **Status**:
  - Count (three generators against three strand-local invariants, diagonal system; $\Sigma_3$ forced; $S_3$ outside the image; one $\chi$ suffices; $b_\chi^2/a_\chi=4$ undisturbed; $\beta(c_6)$ hand-derived): Conditional(PH14 [$O(g_s^4)$, $O(g_s^5)$], PH15, PH16) – Proven (combinatorial count, 038 §3–§5; numerical consequence checks)
  - Matching appendix (048: deficit $-V_7(\Psi^7)-\tfrac{42}{25}S_1+\tfrac{7(2+c_6)}5S_2$, closure at $(c_\chi,d_\chi,a_7)=(1,2+c_6,1)$, six of seven tests): Conditional(PH14 [$O(g_s^4)$, $O(g_s^5)$], PH15, PH16) – Numerical (random-tensor model, three seeds); C6 open

**Can the $\chi$ scheme reach $O(g_s^5)$ with elementary couplings? A paper count (derivation 038).** A counting argument, not a matching computation. With field content $(\Psi,\Sigma_2,\chi,\Sigma_3)$ and **bare geometric (elementary) vertices only**, the $O(g_s^5)$ matching has exactly enough freedom to close: the order formula $\mathrm{ord}=(\sum_is_i-2)+\sum_io_i$ (strand conservation from R23 plus saddle orders $o(\Psi)=o(\Sigma_2)=o(\Sigma_3)=0$, $o(\chi)=1$) gives three generators — $V_7\rvert_{331}/V_7\rvert_{322}\to V_7(\Psi^7)$, $V_5(\chi,\chi,\Psi)\to S_1$, $V_6(\chi,\Sigma,\Sigma)\to S_2$ (with $V_6(\chi,\Sigma_3,\Psi)$ a redundant partner) — against the three strand-local order-7 invariants $\{V_7(\Psi^7),S_1,S_2\}$ that an all-elementary presentation can generate, and the induced $3\times3$ system is triangular, in fact diagonal. Further: $\Sigma_3$ with its even HS pair at $O(g_s^4)$ is **forced in the $\chi$ scheme too** (on $(\Psi,\Sigma_2,\chi)$ no cubic coupling reaches 7 strands with an $O(1)$ saddle); the strand-split invariant $S_3$ ($\mathcal C$-type) lies **outside the image of any all-elementary presentation** (strand-locality lemma), which is why it does not enter the deficit; **one $\chi$ suffices** at this order (every $\chi_3$ coupling lands at $O(g_s^6)$ unless given an $O(g_s^4)$ source, in which case it degenerates into a second $\Sigma_3$); and the $O(g_s^4)$ relation $b_\chi^2/a_\chi=4$ (R24) is **undisturbed**, the two new $\chi$ couplings contributing first at $O(g_s^5)$. The order-7 coefficient of the deficit is $\beta(c_6)=\tfrac{7(2+c_6)}5$, hand-derived. Conditions: $b'_\chi=0$ (no $V_5(\chi,\Sigma_3)$ mixing, condition (E4)) — with $b'_\chi\neq0$ a fourth invariant $S_4$ appears, the system is no longer diagonal and that branch has not been run in 038 (048 runs it only as a break leg, C6, open); and the sextic contact split $c_6+c_{\Sigma_3}=1$, which is $O(g_s^5)$-visible and is *pinned by convention* to $c_6=0$, not derived, so the fitted coefficient values are quoted at that split. What this is **not**: not the matching computation — the values of the two new couplings require it (048, matching appendix below); and the count is order by order, saying nothing about $O(g_s^6)$ and beyond. Reproduces R20, R24 and 018 §5(a) as falsifier checks. **Matching appendix (derivation 048, 2026-09-22; C6 left open):** in the random-tensor model on three seeds, six of the seven tests of 038 §6.2 are established – with the deficit taken as action minus target, $7!\times$ the $O(g^5)$ coefficient of $S^\chi_5(\text{saddle})-S_{\rm eff}$ is $-V_7(\Psi^7)-\tfrac{42}{25}S_1+\tfrac{7(2+c_6)}{5}S_2$ (R49), no $S_3$ and no orthogonal remainder (N3, N3′), the $3\times3$ system diagonal, closure at $(c_\chi,d_\chi,a_7)=(1,2+c_6,1)$ with residual $O(g_s^6)$, $\Sigma_3$-kinetic independence (N4), lower legs untouched, $\beta(c_6)$ affine as predicted. The seventh, the $b'_\chi$ falsification leg C6, is **open**: its out-of-span coefficient is an $O(g_s^5)$ quantity but is dominated by a composite-dressing structure (048 eq. (8.3), not yet reviewed) rather than by the $\Sigma_3$-mixing direction, so it neither establishes nor refutes (E4); separating them needs the explicit $S_4$ column. By-product sharpening (E4): the mixing is $O(g^3)$ against an $O(g^4)$ $\Sigma_3$ kinetic term, so the $\chi$ kinetic term acquires an $M^{-1}$ composite at $O(g_s^2)$ and R24's $O(g_s^4)$ closure fails for every $b_\chi$ once $b'_\chi\ne0$ (048 (8.1)–(8.2), re-derived by the iteration-2 critic).

**Annotation (2026-09-30).** PH23 is discharged (038 Addendum N10); the $O(g^5)$ values of 048 are cited by 038 N10.5 only for the existence of a closure (non-emptiness of the $\lambda$-set); the 'no further condition on $\lambda$' part rests on $GL(2)$ covariance.

**Convention anchor — the ghost-sector translation between this project, the SL(2,C) quartic-vertex project and [Erbin 2023] (derivation 041; companion script).** The three sides use the *same* $c_0^\pm=\tfrac12(c_0\pm\bar c_0)$, $b_0^\pm=b_0\pm\bar b_0$, the same ordering $c_0^-c_0^+$ and the same signs (Belopolsky–Zwiebach eq. 2.7 = `cft:eq:bn-cn-pm`), so the $\nu=-1$ vs $\nu=-\tfrac12$ discrepancy is **not** a $c_0^\pm$ convention: it is the normalisation of the sphere vacuum correlator, declared in the ghost sector by all three sources, $\lambda=\tfrac12$ per correlator (sister → book). Hence, converting sister magnitudes to this project's convention: $V_4\times\tfrac12$ (one sphere correlator), $G_2\times\tfrac14$ (two), $\operatorname{spec}(m_2)\times2$, $C_3,C_4\times\tfrac12$; **signs unchanged**. Physically inert: it is the field/coupling rescaling $S_{\rm book}[\Psi;\kappa]=S_{\rm sister}[\Psi/\sqrt2;\sqrt2\,\kappa]$, i.e. $\Psi_{\rm book}=\sqrt2\,\Psi_{\rm sister}$ and $\kappa_{\rm sister}=\kappa_{\rm BZ}=\sqrt2\,\kappa_{\rm book}$ (corrected 2026-10-01: the coupling relation was previously written inverted; no number depended on it), under which $\operatorname{sign}\nu$, $m_T^2=-4/\alpha'$, $\operatorname{rank}V_4$, the signature of $G_2$, eigenvalue ratios, $\hat\lambda$, $\rho(D^{-1}N)$ and the zeros of $\det V_4(P^2)$ are all invariant. **Scope.** The verification establishes the *sign* of the conversion by two independent routes (the book's vacuum normalisation plus mode algebra; the sister's kinetic anchor, which uses no vacuum normalisation); the *magnitude* $\lambda=\tfrac12$ is **confirmed by the Stage-0 recomputation (record 046, 2026-09-21)**: $\{T^3\}(\beta)=2\nu\beta^6$ gives $-\beta^6$ here against the companion's raw value, ratio exactly $\tfrac12$ with equal signs at $\beta=\tfrac32$ and $2$, derived from the book's vacuum normalisation and the cubic mapping radii **without using $\lambda$**, with the $\lambda$-weight (one sphere correlator) and the geometric factor $J=\beta^6$ checked separately — so the $\beta^6$ predicted at 041 §3.2 is confirmed, not restated. What remains declarative, and no computation can change, is $\lambda$'s *definition* as the ratio of the two vacuum normalisations. The absence of a further convention (matter normalisation, volume factor, coupling normalisation) is established for $n\le4$ by the uniform ratio $2$ of pairing, $C_3$ and $C_4$ (046, 062; PH24 discharged 2026-10-01). **Not** covered here: Q2, the measure orientation $(2\pi\mathrm i)^{-1}$ vs $\mathrm i/2\pi$ per complex modulus — **resolved since, by R47** (042: the two transcriptions do not conflict, $\chi=\kappa\mathrm i\eta=-1$ in each source's own contour convention); a three-point function cannot probe it in any case (046 §6).

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/043-gap3-representative-freedom.md` (flat representative $\hat R_u$, trivial-symmetry characterisation, $G^{\rm T}K_\Psi G=p_{2,2}$); `agent/derivations/040-gauge-algebra-ogs3.md` §5 (exact $\Sigma_2$ law, $\mathcal E$)

*Gauge algebra at $O(g_s^3)$, $\Sigma_2$ row — the defect is representative-dependent and removable.* With the **exact** $\Sigma_2$ law R17/011 (B.8)–(B.9), $[\delta_{\Lambda_1},\delta_{\Lambda_2}]\Sigma_2=\delta_{\Lambda_{12}}\Sigma_2+\Phi_{\rm exact}(\mathcal D_\Psi)+\mathcal E$ with the **same** $\Lambda_{12}$ and no new gauge parameter, where $\mathcal E=\{(\partial_\Psi R)_{[u_2}[u_{1]}]+[R_{u_2},R_{u_1}]\}\tilde X$ is **exactly** $\tilde X$-proportional and equals $\tfrac{g_s^2}{100}[A_{u_1},A_{u_2}]\tilde X+O(g_s^3)$, $A_w:=p_{2,2}^{-1}p_{3,2}(\cdot,\cdot,w)$. **$\mathcal E$ is the curvature of $R_u$ read as a connection on field space, and it is REMOVABLE (043, GAP-3 closed).** Invariance of $S_3$ fixes only $\operatorname{Sym}(K_\Psi R_u)=\tfrac{g_s}{10}p_{3,2}(\cdot,\cdot,u)$, so the admissible freedom is exactly $\Delta R_u=K_\Psi^{-1}S_u$ with $S_u$ antisymmetric — which is exactly the set of Henneaux–Teitelboim **trivial** symmetries for even fields, complementary to R22, whose *forced* part has symmetric $\mu=-\tfrac{6}{5g_s}K_\Psi^{-1}W_{\delta\Psi}K_\Psi^{-1}$ (R22 untouched). The flat admissible representative is $\hat R_u=(\partial_uM_\Psi^{-1/2})M_\Psi^{1/2}$, $M_\Psi=1-\tfrac{g_s}5A_\Psi$: it satisfies the invariance condition and gives $\mathcal E[\hat R]=0$ identically — **an exact identity in $g_s$ for the quintic-truncated action $S_3$**, whose entire $\tilde X$-dependence is the quadratic form $K_\Psi(\tilde X,\tilde X)$ (011 (B.2)) — and differs from R17's choice (the symmetric logarithmic derivative $-\tfrac12M_\Psi^{-1}\partial_uM_\Psi$) by the trivial symmetry $-\tfrac{g_s^2}{200}[A_\Psi,A_u]+O(g_s^3)$. Equivalently $G^{\rm T}K_\Psi G=p_{2,2}$ for $G=M_\Psi^{-1/2}$: in $\hat Y=M_\Psi^{1/2}\tilde X$ the auxiliary kinetic form is $\Psi$-independent and $\delta\hat Y=0$ — the **form** of R11's $\delta X=0$, in a variable that reduces to R11's $X$ only as $g_s\to0$. **Hence in the preferred representative the algebra closes modulo the $\Psi$ equation of motion alone**; "closes modulo **both** equations of motion" is a property of the R17/011 (B.8) representative, not of the theory, and $\lambda^\Sigma$ is a trivial-symmetry coboundary rather than a genuine $E$-structure function. **Carve-outs:** (i) established within $S_3$; at $O(g_s^4)$ ($\mathcal V_6$, $\Sigma_3$) the premise "$\tilde X$ enters only through $K_\Psi(\tilde X,\tilde X)$" must be re-established first; (ii) algebraic layer, formal power series, PH1/PH5 (invertibility of $p_{2,2}$ and $K_\Psi$ on the quotient); (iii) the verdict does **not** inherit GAP-2's $N=2$ limit — it uses only total symmetry of the vertices and invertibility — and GAP-2 now bears only on whether R17's representative is curved at all; (iv) the BV/antifield consequence ($(\hat Y,\hat Y^+)$ as a constant-coefficient contractible pair, the canonical transformation induced on $\Psi^+$, the inheritance by 015 §4) is **expected, not derived** here; derived since by R60 (annotation 2026-09-26 below); (v) the analogous question for the $\mathcal F$-proportional $\mathcal D_\Psi$ is untouched: the openness of the closed-SFT gauge algebra (Zwiebach 1993) stands. Supersedes the "modulo both equations of motion" reading of the 2026-09-22 row and closes its GAP-3. **Annotation 2026-09-26 (R57):** the $O(g_s^4)$ re-opening (GAP-3d) is closed — with $W_6$ present a flat admissible representative exists (Morse map plus square root) and the algebra closes modulo the $\Psi$ EOM alone within $S_4$; carve-out (v) unchanged. *Annotation 2026-09-26 (GAP-3a):* the flatness of $\hat R$ is machine-checked at $N=3$ through $g_s^5$ as well (043 part D, 54/54 PASS), in addition to the analytic all-orders proof and the $N=2$ check.

**Annotation (2026-09-26).** (R60): carve-out (iv) closed — the flat representative's redefinition extends to a canonical transformation, the $\hat Y^+$ term vanishes in the hatted chart, and R45's $\lambda^\Sigma$ is the trivial symmetry generated by $\Xi=\tfrac12Y^+\mu_cY^+$ (classical leading-order identity machine-checked; BV normalisation at antifield number two not tracked); conditional on the $\Psi$-sector master equation (Task 7), established since to the truncation order by R62 ($k\le4$, under R62's conditions, PH27 among them).

**Annotation (2026-09-30, GAP-3c; 043 addendum C5).** The flat admissible representative is unique up to trivial symmetries. A $\Psi$-independent relabelling of the inert variable $\hat Y$, orthogonal or not, leaves the law unchanged. The flat admissible laws are exactly $F^h_u=-(D(h\circ\mathcal G))^{-1}\partial_u(h\circ\mathcal G)$ with $h_\Psi$ a $\Psi$-dependent $p_{2,2}$-preserving map, $h_0=\mathrm{id}$, subject to the ghost-number, reality, algebraicity and $O(g_s)$ conditions of the law. In the linear sector, $H_\Psi\in\mathrm O(p_{2,2})$, equivalently $\hat R\to g\hat Rg^{-1}+(\partial g)g^{-1}$ with $g_\Psi$ $K_\Psi$-orthogonal. Each member differs from $\hat R$ by a pure-gauge Henneaux–Teitelboim trivial symmetry $K_\Psi^{-1}S_u$, $S_u$ antisymmetric; the higher-degree terms of the law are field-dependent trivial symmetries $K_\Psi^{-1}S(\tilde X)\tilde X$ (Koszul). Since $\mathcal E\to g\mathcal Eg^{-1}$ exactly, $\mathcal E=0$ on the whole family: exactly in $g_s$ within $S_3$, and within $S_4$ under PH14 (R57). On R17's curved representative the leading coefficient $\tfrac{g_s^2}{100}[A_{u_1},A_{u_2}]$ is invariant. The BV extensions of any two members are related by an explicit cotangent-lift canonical transformation in R60's chart; properness as in R60 (R62: $k\le3$ under PH27; $k=4$, through $g_s^4$ at generic momentum, under R62's $k=4$ conditions, since 2026-10-01). - **Source**: `agent/derivations/039-reality-ogs3.md` §1–§3, claim tree C1–C4

**Reality of $\Sigma_2$ at $O(g_s^3)$ (closes the 2026-09-19 open action).** With $\Psi^\ddagger=\Psi$ and $\delta\Psi^\ddagger=\delta\Psi$: the corrected saddle $\Sigma_2^*=K_\Psi^{-1}p_{2,2}(\Psi^{\otimes2})$ and the corrected gauge law $\delta\Sigma_2=2\,\delta\Psi\odot\Psi+\tfrac{3g_s}5P(\delta\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac{g_s}{10}P(\Sigma_2-\Psi^{\otimes2},\delta\Psi)+O(g_s^2)$, with $P=p_{2,2}^{-1}p_{3,2}$, both preserve $\Sigma_2^\ddagger=\Sigma_2$ in $W_q=\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0/\ker\mathcal V_4$ — to all orders of the formal $g_s$ expansion (PH1), and non-perturbatively in $g_s$ within the quintic truncation wherever $K_\Psi$ is invertible on $W_q$ (the domain of the statement, not a hypothesis; non-empty by R70). Mechanism: $\mathcal V_4,\mathcal V_5$ $\ddagger$-equivariant $\Rightarrow$ the composites $P=p_{2,2}^{-1}p_{3,2}$ and $R=K_\Psi^{-1}p_{2,2}$ are real maps between even-strand spaces, characterised by $\mathcal V_4(A,PC)=\mathcal V_5(A,C)$ and $\mathcal V_4^\Psi(A,RB)=\mathcal V_4(A,B)$; $\ker\mathcal V_4$ is $\ddagger$-stable and $\ddagger$ descends to $W_q$; all coefficients are real rationals times real $g_s$. **$\ddagger$-stability of $\ker\mathcal V_4$ is a consequence of PH6 at $n=4$ alone** — one line from $\mathcal V_4(A,B^\ddagger)=\mathcal V_4(A^\ddagger,B)^*$ — not a new hypothesis. Reality is insensitive to whether $2\,\delta\Psi\otimes\Psi$ is symmetrised; the choice affects only whether $\delta\Sigma_2\in\mathrm{Sym}^2$. **PH7 is not needed for the statement proved here**, which is about variations already tangent to the real locus and whose load-bearing chain stays on even-strand spaces (no $\ddagger$ is applied to the odd-strand $N_{\rm gh}=6$ target of $p_{2,2}$ and $p_{3,2}$ anywhere in C1); whether every gauge parameter produces such a variation is the odd-sector question, not settled in 039 and answered on 2026-10-01 under C3 (annotation below): every $\Lambda$ with $\Lambda^\ddagger=-\Lambda$ (Zwiebach (3.18)) gives $\delta\Psi^\ddagger=\delta\Psi$, given PH6 in signed form at $n=3,4,5$; PH7 ($\ddagger^2=1$) is discharged. Carve-outs: algebraic layer; PH6 at $n=4,5$; PH1 for the inverse on the quotient; R70 for the non-emptiness of the non-perturbative form's domain; PH5/R38 for the descent to $W_q$ ($N_{\rm gh}(p_{2,2})=+2$ per R23). *Annotation 2026-09-26:* the PH6 [n=4] leg is supported for the SL(2,C) family by R56; the $n=5$ leg stays open.

*Annotation (2026-10-01, node check `agent/reports/critic-r11-r13-c3-20261001T093500.md`; conventions C3):*

- The open item of the Scope is answered. Under C3, $(Q\Lambda)^\ddagger=-Q\Lambda^\ddagger$ and $\ell_n(\Psi^{n-1},\Lambda)^\ddagger=-\ell_n(\Psi^{n-1},\Lambda^\ddagger)$ for real $\Psi$. So every gauge parameter with $\Lambda^\ddagger=-\Lambda$ (Zwiebach (3.18)) produces a variation with $\delta\Psi^\ddagger=\delta\Psi$, which is assumption A4 of record 039; $\Lambda^\ddagger=+\Lambda$ would give an imaginary $\delta\Psi$.
- The conclusion of R46 is unchanged. It extends to every real gauge transformation, on condition of PH6 at $n=3,4,5$ in the signed component form $V_n(A_1^\ddagger,\dots)=(-1)^{k_o(k_o-1)/2}V_n(A_1,\dots)^*$ on two Grassmann-odd arguments (the $\ell_2,\ell_3,\ell_4$ terms of $\delta\Psi$).
- Record 039 §3 Commentary's "iff $\Lambda^\ddagger=\Lambda$ … under PH7" has the sign of Zwiebach's constant-sign conjugation, not of C3, and its input is the signed PH6, not PH7.

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/042-q2-measure-orientation.md` §4, §5, §6–§7, claim tree Root R, N1–N5; the value $\chi=-1$ (N6): sister `SL: R37`, imported (042 §6.1)

**Q2 resolved — the BZ and book measure-prefactor transcriptions do not conflict; the apparent $(-1)^{N-3}$ disagreement (042 §1) was a reading mismatch, not a fact (042 §6).** The on-shell reduction gives $\int_{\mathcal S_{0,N}}\omega_{2(N-3)}\rvert_{\rm on\text{-}shell}=(\kappa\,\mathrm i\,\eta/2\pi)^{N-3}A^{\rm FP}_{0,N}$ (042 (4.8), (5.2), Root R), so only $\chi:=\kappa\,\mathrm i\,\eta\in\{\pm1\}$ is observable and the four-reading table of 042 §4 is two claims, not four, fixed by the two independent binary choices $\kappa$ (the printed prefactor) and $\eta$ (the $\oint\dd\bar w/2\pi\mathrm i$ contour convention, 042 (4.1)). The book's own stated contour rule, taken literally, gives $\eta=-1$ — confirmed in print by de Lacroix, Erbin, Kashyap, Sen, Verma (2017), arXiv:1703.06410 App. B (042 §6.2, claim-tree N6 closed) — hence $\chi=-1$, the **same** value the SL(2,C) project obtains from BZ's prefactor read with its own O3 convention ($\eta=+1$, 039). **No erratum is needed in the book**, only an explicit statement of its $\oint\dd\bar w/2\pi\mathrm i$ convention next to `bos:eq:omega-p` (text proposed, 042 §7). For this project, Q2 stays nearly inert (041): with $\chi=-1$, branch A (BZ, $C_4<0$ potential side) is the one in force, and R15 clause (3)'s "sign open" closes as $C<0$, so the unconditional Schwinger form $\int_0^\infty\dd t\,m_2\,\e^{-tm_2^2}$ is the one to use (042 §7). **Scope caveat, load-bearing: the numeric value $\chi=-1$ is anchored to `SL: R37` and is not re-derived here** (042 §6.1, claim-tree N6) — only the identity $\chi=\kappa\mathrm i\eta$, the reading of the four-way table, and the book/BZ agreement are established by this derivation. *Annotation 2026-09-26 (Task 9i, `agent/context/r47-chi-crosscheck-2026-09-26.md`):* the external value is numerically equal to this row's $\chi$ but obtained by an unrelated argument; 042 §6.1 states the inheritance as such — a well-posed comparison, no convention gap.

### R48 — Off-shell four-tachyon contact term in the SL(2,C) section depends on momenta only through $(s,t,u)$ [Conditional(PH29, PH30) – Verified]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/044-stu-reduction-contact-term.md` Blocks 1–6, claim tree C1–C5; A4 = sister `SL: R51` (mapping-radii closed form), A5 = sister `SL: R6`/`SL: R10` (equivariant tile assembly), imported, not re-derived here
- **Scope**: tachyons only (dressing exponent A7); the contact term only, not the $s/t/u$ exchange contributions; the declared section ($S=S_0$) with $\beta>\beta_{04}$, exercised numerically at $\beta\in\{3/2,2\}$; a statement about the integrand, not about the tractability of the resulting kernel (record 045); does not establish A4 or A5, nor determine $\rho_3$ independently of the Klein relation (044 C5.4)
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-21 – exact check – `codes/verify-stu-reduction.py`: eq. (1.6) as an exact sympy identity plus numerical checks against the sister module; 32/32 PASS, 0 FAIL, 5 break legs firing, seed 20260921, exit 0, re-run by the orchestrator
  - 2026-09-21 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-044-20260921T220500.md` – REVISE, 0 Fatal / 3 Serious, all on claimed-versus-actual verification (dead code, a check that cannot fail, break-leg count) → `agent/reports/fix-044-20260921T223500.md`, mechanical, no number changed
  - 2026-09-21 – independent re-derivation – critic-044 – Blocks 1, 2, 4, 6 re-derived and confirmed, including the $S_4/V$ statement element by element
  - 2026-09-23 – numerical check – SL(2,C) project's full machinery (reduced and unreduced 12-tile routes, radii from the explicit maps) – agrees with $F$ at eight points to $\le2.9\times10^{-9}$; supports A4 at those points, does not test A5, $C_4$, $\lambda$ or a residual $\rho_3$ factor
  - 2026-09-26 – critic review – critic-ledger-K1 and critic-ledger-K4 – PH26 discharged → R54; condition dropped

**The off-shell four-tachyon contact term of the SL(2,C) declared section depends on the external momenta only through $(s,t,u)$ (derivation 044).** In that section the individual $k_a^2$ cancel **pointwise in the moduli parameter $Z$ and per tile**, using only $\sum_ak_a=0$ and no mass-shell input: the mapping radii obey $\rho_a=c(Z)\prod_{b\ne a}\lvert z_{ab}\rvert$ with $c(Z)=\beta^{-1}(\lvert Z\rvert\lvert1-Z\rvert)^{-1/2}$ **independent of $a$**, so the dressing $\prod_a\rho_a^{\alpha'k_a^2/2}$ cancels the $k_a^2$-dependent part of the regulated Koba–Nielsen factor and leaves $c(Z)^{\alpha'(s+t+u)/2}\lvert Z\rvert^{\alpha'u/2}\lvert1-Z\rvert^{\alpha't/2}$. An **iff**: it holds exactly when $c(Z)$ is $a$-independent, since $c\to c\,e^{\gamma_a}$ leaves $\exp[\tfrac12\alpha'\sum_a\gamma_ak_a^2]$. With $\prod_a\rho_a=\beta^{-4}$ (`SL: R5`, recovered not assumed) the 12-tile sum collapses to $2C\beta^{8-\alpha'(s+t+u)/2}\sum_{S_3}\int_{\mathrm{FD}\cap\mathcal V_{0,4}}\lvert Z\rvert^{\alpha'(x-y-z)/4}\lvert1-Z\rvert^{\alpha'(y-x-z)/4}\mathrm d^2Z$ — totally crossing symmetric and entire in the momenta, the symmetry **derived** ($S_4/V\cong S_3$ faithful on $\{s,t,u\}$, its six cosets realising the six ordered Mandelstam pairs once each). Consequence: at fixed $P^2=s$ the pair kernel $K_P(k;k')$ is a function of two invariants, not four virtualities, with an exact $t\leftrightarrow u$ crossing check on any discretisation — the grid needed for a comparison drops from six parameters to three. Wider than expected: survives **any** second-point map $S$ and is exactly $\Lambda_1$-independent ($\Lambda_1$ enters $R_{\rm interior}$, not the radii). **Negative result carried by this row:** it does **not** close the open gap on $\rho_3$ in the comparison implementation (closed separately by R54, annotation below) — that implementation obtains $\rho_3$ through the Klein relation $F_3:=Z/F_1$, so every check runs against that definition and cannot detect a momentum-independent weight folded into it; a weight common to all four punctures rescales by $\mu^{\alpha'(s+t+u)/2-8}$ and is invisible to every momentum-structure test. **Trap recorded:** on $\alpha'(s+t+u)=16$ that exponent vanishes identically, so a common-weight test at the natural on-shell kinematics reports 'no effect' for the wrong reason (found when a break leg failed to fire; now run at $\alpha'(s+t+u)=6$, a 73 % shift).

**Annotation (2026-09-26).** Closed by derivation 052 (R54): the Klein relation is the $(13)(24)$ symmetry of the vertex, re-derived from the plumbing of two cubic vertices; no residual factor; PH26 discharged on 2026-09-26 (registered 2026-09-23)

**Annotation (2026-10-01).** The measure written $d^2Z$ in this entry and its source is $dx\,dy$ (record 062 §1, critic-062: $\tfrac1{2\pi}d^2z=\tfrac1\pi dx\,dy$ in the Virasoro–Shapiro target); the on-shell value of this contact term enters R43/PH24's $C_4$ ratio through record 062.
