### R50 — $J(p,q)$: boundary representation closes on $p=0$, $q=0$, $(-1,-1)$; generic case a double series [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/047-j-integral-closed-form.md` claim tree C1–C4, Block A, `### Scope of the exclusion`, `## The lines $p=0$ and $q=0$ with coefficients (2026-09-23)`
- **Scope**: mathematics of $J$ over $\mathrm{FD}\cap\mathcal V_{0,4}$ for real $(p,q)$ and $\beta\ge\beta_{04}$ (the four-arc region); the negative is within the class "closed forms reachable by the boundary route (A.2) as finite combinations of ${}_2F_1$ and Appell $F_1/F_2$", for generic $(p,q)$, and is not a proof of non-existence; single arcs close on further lines without closing $J$ (047 `### Scope of the exclusion`); relevance to $K_P$ inherits PH29, PH30; the Chebyshev evaluation is numerical at $\lvert p\rvert+\lvert q\rvert\le170$, $\beta\in\{\tfrac32,2\}$, without a rigorous error bound; the series route (c′) is open (047 C4.1 [GAP]); weakest relaxation: a contiguity or quadratic-transformation reduction of (A.9) at the arc endpoints, or a closed form for the triangle piece of (B.5) (047 *Scope of the exclusion*)
- **Origin**: the `Argued` parts – `agent/derivations/047-j-integral-closed-form.md`, as carried by `agent/reports/general-purpose-ledger-F-20260926T133000.md`
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-22 – numerical check – `codes/verify-j-closed-form.py`, first harness (C1–C4 checks; count not found in the files read) – PASS, "the harness [is] correct" per critic-047 iteration 1
  - 2026-09-22 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-047-20260922T164500.md` – REVISE, mechanical (obstruction re-framed; identity, branches, per-arc results, bipolar representation confirmed) → `agent/reports/computator-047-fixpass-20260922T173000.md`
  - 2026-09-22 – node check – `agent/reports/critic-047-iter2-20260922T184000.md` – obstruction "fails as worded" (a ${}_3F_2$ antiderivative exists at any endpoint; real obstruction is the second power); exclusion wider than its class (closes on $p=0$, $q=0$) → `agent/reports/computator-047-fixpass2-20260922T190500.md`, exclusion restricted to generic $(p,q)$
  - 2026-09-23 – independent re-derivation – independent re-implementation of the representation, agreement $10^{-13}$ at 16 exponent pairs (`agent/reports/computator-047-corr-20260923T130000.md`)
  - 2026-09-23 – numerical check – `codes/verify-j-closed-form.py` after `agent/reports/computator-047-c24-20260923T124500.md`: 25/25 PASS, exit 0, seed 20260922, 11 break legs firing in 9 checks; `C6-closed-lines` verifies (L.6), (L.12) at 16 points to $1.4\times10^{-15}$
  - 2026-09-23 – node check – `agent/reports/critic-047-corr-20260923T141000.md` – accept items 1, 2, 3, 5, the range half of item 8, (L.6), (L.12); $1/(\lambda+1)$ prefactor added to (A.11) → `agent/reports/computator-047-fix-20260923T142500.md`
  - 2026-09-23 – node check – `agent/reports/critic-047-improve-20260923T155000.md` (consolidation) – accept for promotion after a count fix → `agent/reports/computator-047-fix2-20260923T161000.md`
- **Status**:
  - boundary representation $J=\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}\oint_{\partial R}\overline G\,\mathrm dG$, constant-phase criterion per arc, closed forms on $p=0$, $q=0$ and at $(-1,-1)$, the reduction of generic arcs to a Kampé de Fériet double series, the confocal trapezoid, the $n^{-3}$ convergence of `j_integral`: Proven (047 C1, C2.1–C2.4, C3.1, C4)
  - "no closed form by the boundary route for generic $(p,q)$": Argued (047 C2.3: "Not a proof of non-existence"; the ellipse step "argument, not machine-checked")
  - hypergeometric-free Chebyshev evaluation and its accuracy: Numerical (regime under `**Scope**`)

**The tile integral $J(p,q)=\int_{\mathrm{FD}\cap\mathcal V_{0,4}}\lvert Z\rvert^p\lvert1-Z\rvert^q\,\mathrm d^2Z$ of R48 has an exact one-dimensional boundary representation; it closes on the lines $p=0$, $q=0$ and at $(-1,-1)$, and for generic $(p,q)$ has no closed form by the boundary route (derivation 047).** $J=\tfrac12\operatorname{Im}\oint_{\partial R}\overline G\,\mathrm dG$ with $G=B_Z(\tfrac p2+1,\tfrac q2+1)-B_{-1}(\cdot)$ the incomplete Beta (principal branches, boundary values from above on the negative axis): the area of $G(R)$, valid for $\beta\ge\beta_{04}=\tfrac12+\tfrac1{\sqrt2}$ (the four-arc region). An arc closes by constant phase iff $\arg(f\,\mathrm dZ)$ is constant on it: real segment always ($0$), vertical segment iff $p=q$, unit circle iff $2p+q=-4$ (contribution exactly $0$), ellipse iff $p=q=-1$ (a triangle in two endpoint values of $G$). On $p=0$ and $q=0$ the route closes with all coefficients: $J(0,q)$ and $J(p,0)$ are finite combinations of ${}_2F_1$ (incomplete Betas on the circle and vertical arcs; the circle elementary on $q=0$) and two Appell $F_1$ on the ellipse arc through R2's uniformiser $Z=Z_4^{(s)}(1/w)=(4\beta^2w+1)^2/16\beta^2w$ (first argument $c-\mathrm i0$ at $w=-1$ on $q=0$), verified at 16 points to $1.4\times10^{-15}$ (`C6-closed-lines`); the two lines are not related by a symmetry of $R$. $J(-1,-1)$ closes in ${}_2F_1$ endpoint values and one ${}_3F_2$. For generic $(p,q)$ — off these lines and this point — circle and ellipse arcs stay one-dimensional integrals of (two powers)$\times{}_2F_1$ with unmatched exponents: a Kampé de Fériet double series in one variable (antiderivative $\tfrac{z^{\lambda+1}}{\lambda+1}\sum_{m,n}\dots$, prefactor included), single-series when Euler's transformation absorbs a power ($\mu=a+b-c$; sufficient, not necessary: $p=0$ and $q=-2$, one per Pfaff representative, $q=-1$ directly, and the degenerate $p+q=-4$ where $G$ is elementary; circle arc only). The obstruction is not a missing symmetry — every arc carries an algebraic Schwarz function ($1/Z$, $1-Z$, $\tfrac12+4(a^2+b^2)(Z-\tfrac12)\pm8ab\sqrt{Z(Z-1)}$ on the confocal ellipse, branch points at the foci $0,1$ since $a^2-b^2=\tfrac14$), so reflection holomorphises $\overline G$ on the three curved arcs (checked to $10^{-29}$) — nor the endpoints (one power against a ${}_2F_1$ integrates to a ${}_3F_2$ at any endpoint); on the ellipse the ${}_2F_1$ sits moreover at a quadratic-rational argument (argument, not machine-checked). Not a proof of non-existence. In confocal coordinates $u=\lvert Z\rvert+\lvert1-Z\rvert$, $v=\lvert1-Z\rvert-\lvert Z\rvert$ the region is the trapezoid $\{0\le v\le1,\ 2+v\le u\le2a\}$ with elementary weight; the unit-circle side is the only non-coordinate boundary. Hypergeometric-free evaluation by Chebyshev cumulative integration along $\partial R$: $\sim2400$ evaluations, agreement with the ${}_2F_1$ evaluation $\lesssim10^{-10}$ (worst of 12 points, $10^{-15}$ at moderate exponents) and with 2D quadrature to its $2\times10^{-9}$ at $\lvert p\rvert+\lvert q\rvert\le170$, $\beta\in\{\tfrac32,2\}$; rigorous error bound not yet quantified. Side findings: `j_integral` of `verify-kp-box.py` converges as $n^{-3}$ (square-root branch point at the panel endpoint $y=1$), $\sim10^{-6}$ at $n=40$; the induced shift of the six-$J$ contact term at 045's eighteen headline points is $\le4\times10^{-7}$ (check `C5-045-shift`), so no 045 conclusion moves. **Consequence for the cross-project comparison: this project evaluates $K_P$ itself; a comparison needs only a few reference points, not a grid.** The derivation belongs to the companion paper (Harold, 2026-09-22); this project cites it.

### R51 — $K_P$ at level 0 is Hilbert–Schmidt self-adjoint; Task 18c well posed only in operator form [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/049-kp-pair-kernel.md` claim tree C1–C3, Block 1 (1.1)–(1.3), 18c decomposition
- **Scope**: level 0, the tachyon-pair block at fixed $P$, on the Euclidean relative-momentum space $L^2_{\rm even}(\mathbb R^{26})$ (PH25); $\beta>\sqrt2$ for the Gaussian bound; self-adjoint at real $s$ only (complex-symmetric at complex $s$); the spectrum is numerical, s-wave, at $\beta=\tfrac32$, $\alpha'P^2\in\{4,16/3\}$ (sister normalisation, $C=+1$)
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-23 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-049-20260923T124600.md` – REVISE, 0 Fatal / 6 Serious → fix pass 2026-09-23 (H1 = PH25 declared on the orchestrator's decision, critic F3)
  - 2026-09-23 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-049-iter2-20260923T134500.md` – Accept with 3 residual findings (two-tile corner model, `/2` growth factor, `C0-Jvec-corner` at $\beta=2$), applied 2026-09-23
  - 2026-09-23 – numerical check – `codes/verify-kp-kernel.py`, final run: 23/23 PASS, 0 FAIL, 6 kernel-level break legs, seed 20260923, exit 0, re-run by the orchestrator (581 s)
  - 2026-09-26 – critic review – critic-ledger-K1 and critic-ledger-K4 – PH26 discharged → R54; condition dropped from both Status lines
- **Status**:
  - operator properties (real-symmetric, $O(26)$-invariant, Gaussian-bounded iff $\beta>\sqrt2$, Hilbert–Schmidt, entire in $s$, Funk–Hecke reduction, $G_2$ constant on the block) and the stand-ins for PH1 (ii), PH18, PH3: Conditional(PH25, PH29, PH30) – Proven (049 C1.1–C1.4, C2)
  - s-wave Nyström spectrum, positivity of the five leading eigenvalues at $C=+1$, design rule: Conditional(PH25, PH29, PH30) – Numerical (049 C3)

**The level-0 tachyon-pair kernel $K_P$ is a Hilbert–Schmidt self-adjoint operator, and Task 18c is well posed only in operator form (derivation 049).** At fixed $P$, $K_P(\ell,\ell')=C\,F(P^2,(\ell+\ell')^2,(\ell-\ell')^2)$ on $L^2_{\rm even}(\mathbb R^{26})$ is real-symmetric, $O(26)$-invariant ($P$ cancels), Gaussian-bounded iff $\beta>\sqrt2$, hence Hilbert–Schmidt (trace class not claimed in 049; established since in R55, from R53's positivity), entire in $s$, with $G_2$ constant on the block (the $L^2$ pairing is the BPZ one); Funk–Hecke reduces it to radial kernels per even partial wave. "$\det V_4^{(0)}(P^2)$" and "invert on the quotient" do not exist for this operator; the stand-ins are: PH1 (ii) ⇐ $\ker K_P(s)=0$ at the external $s$ (sufficient, not equivalent: a null vector opens the $\ker V_4$-stability question, not a refutation); PH18 ⇔ a complex-$s$ zero off the plumbing lattice of an eigenvalue branch whose null vector is non-isotropic in the bilinear form; PH3 ⇐ absolute convergence of the eigen-expansion at the external relative momenta. s-wave Nyström spectrum at $\beta=\tfrac32$, $\alpha'P^2=16/3$, $C=+1$ (sister normalisation, as in the Scope): $4.271166\times10^9$, $1.025805\times10^9$, $2.539275\times10^8$, converged to $5\times10^{-7}$; five leading eigenvalues positive at $C=+1$ at both $P^2\in\{4,16/3\}$ and both $\beta$ (negative for the ledger's $C<0$, R47). Design rule: radial spacing $\le0.4$, $\Lambda\ge12$ ($\beta=\tfrac32$). Sub-tasks 18c.2–18c.9 decomposed in the record. **Annotation 2026-09-25:** positivity does hold at real $s$ (R53), so trace class follows (050 C4, Mercer; critic 2026-09-26, R55) — the 'trace class not claimed' and 049 (1.5)'s 'positivity, which does not hold' are superseded by R53. **Annotation 2026-09-26:** trace class and the absolutely convergent eigen-expansion are now R55 (050 C4, critic 2026-09-26).

**Corner asymptotics of the tile integral: $J(-q-\delta,q)=2\sqrt{2\pi}\,2^{q}\,q^{-3/2}\,[1+O(1/q)]$ for $q\to+\infty$ at fixed $\delta\ge0$ (derivation 049 node C1.6, eqs. (1.8)–(1.9)).** Laplace at the corner $Z=-1$ of $\mathrm{FD}\cap\mathcal V_{0,4}$: linear exponent transverse to the unit circle (rate $q/2$), quadratic along it (rate $q/8$); the $O(1/q)$ coefficient is observed ($\approx4.8-2\delta$), not derived. ### R53 — $\operatorname{sign}(C)\,K_P(s)$ strictly positive definite on $L^2_{\rm even}(\mathbb R^{26})$ at every real $s$ [Conditional(PH25, PH29, PH30) – Proven]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/050-kp-real-s.md` §18c.4, eqs. (4.1)–(4.5), claim tree C3–C3.3
- **Scope**: level 0, the tachyon-pair block; every real $s$, $\beta>\sqrt2$, $C\ne0$ (definite with sign $C$); not covered: complex $s$ (PH18), the level-$(0,1)$ and higher blocks (PH19), the accumulation rate of eigenvalues at $0$
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-23 – numerical check – `codes/verify-kp-real-s.py`, Nyström spectra at $\beta=2$ (050 C3.4) – no eigenvalue below the projection floor $-10^{-13}\mu_{0,0}$ at any sampled real $s$
  - 2026-09-25 – node check – `agent/reports/critic-050-c3-20260925T115500.md` – all nodes hold, four mechanical edits
  - 2026-09-25 – numerical check – `codes/verify-kp-real-s.py --only c4`, orchestrator re-run – 15/15 PASS, 0 FAIL, 4 break legs incl. the tile-flipped indefinite kernel, seed 20260923, exit 0
  - 2026-09-26 – critic review – critic-ledger-K1 and critic-ledger-K4 – PH26 discharged → R54; condition dropped

**$\operatorname{sign}(C)\,K_P(s)$ is strictly positive definite on $L^2_{\rm even}(\mathbb R^{26})$ at every real $s$ (derivation 050 §18c.4, node C3; critic node check 2026-09-25: all nodes hold, four mechanical edits).** Each of R48's six $S_3$ terms is $\int_Rw_i(Z)\phi_{i,Z}(\ell)\phi_{i,Z}(\ell')e^{c_i(Z)\ell\cdot\ell'}d^2Z$ with $w_i,\phi_{i,Z}>0$, $c_i$ real and $2a_i-\lvert c_i\rvert\ge\alpha'(2\ln\beta-\ln2)>0$ on $R$ (the region inequalities $1\le\lvert Z\rvert\le\lvert1-Z\rvert\le2\lvert Z\rvert$); $e^{c\,\ell\cdot\ell'}$ on even functions is a sum of squares of moments (050 (4.2)) and strictness follows by moment determinacy (entire Fourier transform), the fifth term having $c_5\ne0$ off the vertical arc. Hence $\ker K_P(s)=\{0\}$ for every real $s$, $\beta>\sqrt2$, $C\ne0$ — sign$(C)K_P>0$, every $D$, both declared $\beta$; **form (β) of R51 holds and PH1 (ii) is supported at level 0**; no eigenvalue branch $\mu_{n,j}(s)$ has a real zero (18c.6 searches complex $s$ only; note: this excludes $0$ as an eigenvalue, not a branch tending to $0$ with degenerating eigenvectors, critic report); $m_2=G_2^{-1}V_4$ injective with dense range on the level-0 block; a residual weight common to the four punctures (R48's invisible factor) is a separable conjugation and changes nothing; 049 C1.2's caution 'positivity, which does not hold' is reversed. Consequence: R55 (050 §18c.8, node C4, critic 2026-09-26 ACCEPT after a mechanical fix pass): Mercer ⇒ $K_P$ trace class and the eigen-expansion absolutely convergent at every point — PH3 at level 0, in the eigenbasis form (iii) of R51, by proof. PH26 dropped from the conditions on 2026-09-26 (R54). Not covered: complex $s$ (PH18), the level-$(0,1)$ and higher blocks (PH19), the accumulation rate.

**Annotation (2026-09-26).** The opening sentence is to be read with $\operatorname{sign}(C)$, as in the heading; for the ledger's $C<0$, $K_P(s)$ is negative definite.

**Annotation (2026-10-01, after the 072 node check).** The constant $C$ (and $\operatorname{sign}(C)$) of this entry is the **potential-side** constant of R47 ($C_4<0$); the action-side level-0 kernel is $\tfrac12\lvert C\rvert F>0$ (conventions C5; R68's $A>0$ on $TT$). Record 072 first transcribed the potential-side sign as the action's.

### R54 — No residual factor on $\rho_3$ in the SL(2,C) declared section [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/052-rho3-residual-factor.md` §2A–§2D, claim tree C0–C5, §4

Convention list (`knowledge/conventions.md` C1): physical statistics $\epsilon=N_{\rm gh}\bmod2$; $\mathrm{bpz}(c_n)=(-1)^{n-1}c_{-n}$, $\mathrm{bpz}(b_n)=(-1)^nb_{-n}$, hence $\mathrm{bpz}(c_0^\pm)=-c_0^\pm$; order-preserving BPZ conjugation of creation monomials (it does not fix $s$; C1); $\langle c_{-1}\bar c_{-1}c_0^-c_0^+c_1\bar c_1\rangle=-\tfrac12$. Consequences: 002 R2.6 ($s=-1$) is a sign error (annotated in 002; the $k=2$ headline R7 survives since $s^2=1$); R12's carve-out closed; $S_k$-covariance $\omega_k(\pi X,\pi Y)=\operatorname{sgn}(\pi)\omega_k(X,Y)$; $\omega_k$ vanishes on $\mathcal H_0^{\otimes k}\times\mathcal H_0^{\otimes k}$ and the non-degenerate pairing is $\mathrm{Sym}^k\mathcal H_0\times\Lambda^k_{\rm gr}\mathcal H_3$ (Route C: $\omega_k(\sigma_k,\lambda_k)=\omega_k(\lambda_k,\sigma_k)$, $N_{\rm gh}(\lambda_k)=3k$ unchanged); 028's $(-1)^{k(k-1)/2}$ is the distinct order-reversal sign; no sign of the tower action (R26) depends on $\sigma_k$. Open: none (002 step 4′, node-checked 2026-09-30). **Finite-field truncation I — the $(t,\sigma)$ and five-field models (derivation 057).** Finite-field truncation of the cubic HS theory (algebraic layer, generic real symmetric tensors). For $\mathcal U\subset\mathcal H$ and $\mathcal S=\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal U$ with $A=V_4\vert_{\mathcal S}$ invertible, eliminating $\Sigma$ gives: at $S_2$, exactly the level-$\mathcal U$ quartic potential; at $S_3$, a rational potential with denominator $\det K_{\mathcal S}(\Psi)$, equal to $S^{(5)}_{\rm eff}\vert_{\mathcal U}+\tfrac{g^4}{600}v_S^\top A^{-1}v_S+O(g^5)$; at $S_4$, with R19's $W_6$ built on the full $V_4^{-1}$ (not computable inside a truncation), $S^{(6)}_{\rm eff}\vert_{\mathcal U}-\tfrac{g^4}{600}w^\top M^{-1}w+O(g^5)$, the Schur term nonzero for generic tensors, and $w=0$ iff pointwise $V_4^{-1}V_5(\cdot,P,\Psi)\in\mathcal S$ (the scalar $w^\top M^{-1}w$ can also vanish with $w\neq0$, $M$ being indefinite); with the $\mathcal S$-consistent composite $W_6^{(\mathcal S)}=V_6-\tfrac65\mathcal C_{\mathcal S}$ ($A^{-1}$ in place of $V_4^{-1}$; the vertex to use in truncations), $S^{(6)}_{\rm eff}\vert_{\mathcal U}+O(g^5)$ without enlarging $\mathcal S$. One-field model: $\sigma_*=V_4t^2/(V_4-\tfrac g5V_5t)$, simple pole at $t_{\rm pole}=5V_4/(gV_5)$ at $S_3$ with no finite saddle there; at $S_4$, on the perturbative branch, the pole is replaced by a fold at $\tau^\pm_{\rm fold}=5V_4/(V_5\pm\sqrt{5W_6V_4})$ (real iff $W_6V_4>0$; for $W_6V_4<0$ a finite saddle exists at $t_{\rm pole}$). All these scales are at $gt=O(1)$ (exact scaling), so the matchings are formal (Taylor coefficients through $\Psi^5$ or $\Psi^6$), not accuracy statements at the tachyon vacuum.

**Classical BV master equation of the cubic HS action with $\Sigma_2$ ($k\le3$) and $\Sigma_2$, $\Sigma_3$ ($k=4$) to the truncation order (derivation 058; closes 056 C2.4 for $k\le4$).** $\Psi$ sector: Zwiebach 1993's classical master action is the classical action at unrestricted ghost number (text after (3.35)); its CME follows from the genus-zero main identity ((4.12)/(4.14); (4.70)–(4.83) at $\hbar=0$; cited), and conversely by polarisation (derived). Truncation lemma (proven): keeping $V_n$, $n\le k+2$, $[g^m](S^{(k)},S^{(k)})$ equals the full action's coefficient $\mathcal J_m$ for $m\le k$, hence vanishes under PH27 (C1); for Zwiebach's truncated action the $g^{k+1}$ coefficient is $-2(S_2,S_{k+3})=\sum_{a+b=k+5,\,3\le a,b\le k+2}(S_a,S_b)$. With R60's contractible pair, $S_{\rm BV}=S^{\Psi,(k)}_{\rm BV}-\tfrac{g_s^2}{4!}p(\hat Y,\hat Y)$, where $S^{\Psi,(k)}_{\rm BV}$ is Zwiebach's truncated action plus $\mathbb F$ (the action R60 uses), solves the CME through $O(g_s^k)$ for $k=2$ (R1) and $k=3$ (R17). $\mathbb F\equiv0$ at $k=2$. At $k=3$, $\mathbb F=\tfrac{g_s^4}{600}vK_\Psi^{-1}v$ enters at $g_s^4$ and adds $2(S_2,\mathbb F^{(6)})$ (not computed) to the obstruction. The obstruction's antifield-free part is the free BRST variation of $S_6-\mathbb F^{(6)}$, which on the ghost-number-2 field is $\tfrac1{6!}W_6(\Psi^{\otimes6})$ with R19's $W_6$, plus the free EOM contracted with the $\ell_5(\Psi^4,c)$ gauge term of $S_6$. Premise: the kept HS vertices are the truncation of a genus-zero $L_\infty$ family (PH27; PH4 is its $n=4$ covering clause). 056's minimal sector ($\Psi$, $c$, $u_c$, $f(c,c)$, open-algebra term) is the $G\in\{1,2\}/\{3,4\}$ part of Zwiebach's field, completed by the ghost-for-ghost tower. Mechanism machine-checked in a finite cyclic $L_\infty$ model (tree-level transfer of matrix Chern–Simons, $l_3,l_4,l_5\neq0$).

At $k=4$ (addendum §7): with both auxiliaries, in the chart of 056 (5.1) reached by the exact sequential saddle shifts $\Sigma_3$-then-$\Sigma_2$ (the action is quadratic in $\Sigma_3$ with a $\Psi$-independent kernel, then quadratic in $\Sigma_2$) followed by the flat representative, $S_{\rm BV}=Z^{(4)}+\mathbb F^{\rm dr}-\tfrac{g_s^2}{4!}p(\hat Y_2,\hat Y_2)-\tfrac{g_s^4}{6!}\tilde M(Y_3,Y_3)$ solves the CME through $O(g_s^4)$ under PH27 at its registered scope (the arity-5 relation $\mathcal J_4=0$ with the $V_6$ of the action; no $V_7$ clause) and is proper through $g_s^4$ at generic momentum. The $g_s^5$ coefficient is $2(S_3,S_6)+2(S_4,S_5)+2(S_2,\mathbb F^{(7)})$, $\mathbb F^{(7)}$ the $g_s^5$ coefficient of $\mathbb F^{\rm dr}$ (explicit as R25's $A_5^{\rm dr}$, under PH14 [$O(g_s^5)$]), equal to $-2(S_2,S_7)+2(S_2,\mathbb F^{(7)})$ iff a consistent $V_7$ exists; $(S_2,\mathbb F^{(7)})$ not computed. The two pairs are constant-coefficient contractible pairs; their only coupling, the bilinear source $\tfrac{2g_s^4}{6!}\tilde u(\Sigma_2)\cdot\Sigma_3$ ($\Psi$-dependent), is removed exactly by the $\Sigma_3$ shift with $\Sigma_2$ off shell, and in the resulting chart $s\hat Y_2=sY_3=0$, so that 056's lemma (e) – which needs the auxiliary sector antifield-free and $\Psi$-independent – applies; $\delta Y_3=0$ (R21 (1a)) is the one two-field input. $W_6$ enters only as the constant $\Sigma_3$ kernel $\tilde M$ ($\tilde M(p_3,p_3)=W_6(\Psi^6)$; the feedback $\tfrac65\mathcal C/6!$ and the contact recombine into $V_6/6!$ in the $\Psi$ sector) and, at $g_s^5$, inside $\mathbb F^{(7)}$. Passing from $k=3$ to $k=4$ adds $2(S_2,W_6/6!)$ (on the ghost-number-2 field) to the $g_s^4$ coefficient, which then vanishes. Finite model with $S_7\neq0$: `codes/verify-bv-master-k4.py`, 45/45 PASS (3 independent + 3 preconditions + 21 break legs – antifield-number-two legs at $g^3$ and $g^4$, one leg per auxiliary pair, a $\Psi$-dependent antifield-free cross term – + 18 bookkeeping, incl. two constant-coefficient pairs and a $\Psi$-independent antifield-free cross term), seeds 11, 12, 13.

**Finite-field truncation II — which untruncated EOM a truncated solution solves (derivation 057 §4).** For $\mathcal S=\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal U$, $A=V_4\vert_{\mathcal S}$ invertible and $V_2(\mathcal U,\mathcal U^\perp)=0$: a solution of the truncated EOM of $S_n$ violates in the untruncated $S_n$ (a) the $\Psi$-EOM along $\mathcal U^\perp$ from $O(g)$, equal to the nonpolynomial level-truncation residual exactly at $S_2$ and through $O(g^2)$ at $S_3,S_4$ (deviation $-\tfrac{g^3}{30}w\cdot(\Psi\odot x)$), and (b), at $S_3,S_4$ only, the $\Sigma$-EOM along $\mathcal S^c$ at relative $O(g)$, $e_c=-\tfrac g5w+O(g^2)$ with R61's $w$; at $S_2$ the $\Sigma$-EOM holds exactly. Relaxing $\mathcal S^c$ at fixed $\Psi$ changes the potential by $\tfrac{g^2}{24}e_c^\top(K_\Psi^{-1})_{cc}e_c$ (exact at $S_3$) $=\tfrac{g^4}{600}w^\top M^{-1}w+O(g^5)$, R61's Schur term; with $W_6^{(\mathcal S)}$ the truncated potential equals R19's fully relaxed one through $O(g^4)$, at the price of (c): the untruncated $\Sigma$-EOM along $\mathcal S$ violated at relative $O(g^2)$ by $\tfrac3{50}(\mathcal C-\mathcal C_{\mathcal S})(P,P,\cdot)$. Under the one-leg universality rule (PH28) with $\mathcal U^\perp$ inside the zero-momentum non-universal space (nonzero momenta drop out by momentum conservation): (a) vanishes identically in the fields; the truncated $S_2$ solution solves the untruncated $S_2$ exactly; at $S_3$ the only violated component is the $\Sigma$-EOM along pairs of non-universal states, sourced by $w$; at $S_4$ also the $\Sigma$-EOM along universal–non-universal pairs at relative $O(g^2)$ through $\mathcal C$; the relaxed configuration violates (a) first at the first unmatched order, $O(g^4)$ at $S_3$, $O(g^5)$ at $S_4$. In the $\mathbb Z_2$ class (a genuine grading, $\mathcal U$ even, $\mathcal U^\perp$ odd, e.g. $X\to-X$ or $\mathbb C/\mathbb Z_2$ with $\mathcal U$ untwisted) every odd component vanishes identically, relaxed configuration included. For a finite $\mathcal U\subsetneq\mathcal H_u$ (tachyon–dilaton) the rule acts towards $\mathcal H_{nu}$ only; along higher universal levels (a) is the ordinary level-truncation error. At the vacuum scale $gt=O(1)$ the order counting is formal; the exact statements are the identities under the rule, the $S_2$ exactness and the relaxation energy.

**Cubic HS theory at $O(g_s^2)$ in the five-field truncation (record 059).** Cubic HS theory at $O(g_s^2)$ in the five-field truncation $\Psi\in\mathrm{span}\{T,D\}$ (level (1,1), zero momentum), $\Sigma\in\mathrm{Sym}^2\{T,D\}$, with the SL(2,C) declared-section data in book normalisation. (i) The elimination of $\Sigma$ is exact at this order: $\Sigma_*=\Psi^{\otimes2}$, i.e. $\sigma_*=(t^2,2td,d^2)$ in the basis $(TT,\tfrac12(TD+DT),DD)$, needing only $\det A\neq0$ (checked numerically), and the effective action is the ordinary two-field quartic closed-SFT potential (R61 with real data): nothing specific to the cubic formulation enters before $V_5$. (ii) The auxiliary quadratic form $A=V_4|_{\mathrm{Sym}^2\{T,D\}}$ is field-independent at this order, with signature $(2,1)$ at $\beta=\tfrac32$ and $2$; at any critical point the joint $(t,d,\sigma)$ Hessian inertia is $\mathrm{In}(A)+\mathrm{In}(\mathrm{Hess}\,\mathcal V)$ (Haynsworth), $(3,2)$ at the $t,d>0$ critical point of record 059, so no critical point can show up as a signature change of the auxiliary form before $K_{\mathcal S}=A-\tfrac g5W(\Psi)$, which needs $V_5(T^{5-k}D^k)$. (iii) At that critical point the quartic energy $p^\top Ap$ is carried by $A$'s negative eigendirection ($\approx TD$, overlap $0.988$, $0.999$), while $\sigma_*$ itself is not dominated by it (a tie with the small positive $\approx DD$ direction at $\beta=\tfrac32$, mainly along it at $\beta=2$). (iv) The dilaton is not a correction: the pure-tachyon restriction has no nontrivial critical point, so the $t,d>0$ critical point exists only through the $D$-dependent couplings, with $\lvert d_*\rvert>\lvert t_*\rvert$ and the $D$-dependent terms cancelling most of the pure-tachyon energy at both $\beta$ (values in record 059 §4, declared section); the dilaton valley is lifted at order $\delta^4$ in $\mathcal V=-S$, a residual exactly cancelled by the level-(2,2) exchange that the truncation drops (sister $d^4$ identity), so the valley critical points are truncation artefacts. The critical-point values, section band, $\beta=2$ fold and the Yang–Zwiebach / Moeller–Yang comparison are recorded in record 059 §3, §5 and routed to the SL(2,C) project. Status: Verified (51/51; exact for (i), (ii)'s Haynsworth relation and (iv)'s residual identity, numerical for the signatures and for (iii), whose figures are asserted by check `C4.4`; the $\lvert d_*/t_*\rvert$ and cancellation figures of §4 are printed, not asserted, and are kept out of the row), no PH.

**Annotation (2026-10-06).** The order-$d^5$ analogue of (iv) is R89: the five-field truncation's dilaton coefficient at $d^5$ is nonzero (Conditional(PH27, SL: PH5)), the dropped level-(2,2) $f_1$ exchange, as the $\delta^4$ residual is; adding the single field $f_1=c_{-1}\bar c_{-1}\lvert0\rangle$ makes the $d^4$ and $d^5$ coefficients exact (cited from the sister's R63, R64; record 095 C5).

### R65 — Mean field I: the joint Hessian of the cubic action is congruent to $\operatorname{Hess}V_{\rm eff}\oplus(-\tfrac{g^2}{12}\mathcal K)$; the Schur identity [Proven – Verified (finite model)]

- **Scope**: finite cubic HS model (generic real symmetric tensors, $\mathcal S$ Veronese-closed, $V_4\vert_{\mathcal S}$ invertible), wherever $\mathcal K$ is invertible; matchings formal in $g$; at the vertices the identities hold where $\mathcal K$ is invertible and the on-shell matchings are Conditional(PH1, PH5) through R1, R17, R19 (PH14 not used)

**Mean field I — fluctuations (record 060, C1).** In the finite cubic HS model (generic real symmetric tensors, $\mathcal S$ Veronese-closed, $V_4\vert_{\mathcal S}$ invertible), the joint Hessian $H$ of $S_n$ at $(\Psi_0,\Sigma_0)$ has blocks (1.2)–(1.4), with $H_{\Sigma\Sigma}=-\tfrac{g^2}{12}\mathcal K$ ($\mathcal K=K_{\Psi_0}$ at $S_3$). Wherever $\mathcal K$ is invertible, $H=L^\top(H_{\rm eff}\oplus H_{\Sigma\Sigma})L$, with $L$ block-unipotent and $H_{\rm eff}$ the Schur complement over $\delta\Sigma$ (1.15). Hence $\mathrm{In}(H)=\mathrm{In}(H_{\rm eff})+\mathrm{In}(-\mathcal K)$, the zero modes of $H$ are those of $H_{\rm eff}$, and $\det H=\det(-\tfrac{g^2}{12}\mathcal K)\det H_{\rm eff}$: congruent, not isospectral. At $\Sigma_0=\Sigma_*(\Psi_0)$, on any branch of the $\Sigma$-equation with $\mathcal K$ invertible, $H_{\rm eff}=\mathrm{Hess}\,V_{\rm eff}(\Psi_0)$ exactly; here $V_{\rm eff}(\Psi)=S_n(\Psi,\Sigma_*(\Psi))$ is action-side (record 060's name), the opposite sign of R64's and R72's potential-side $\mathcal V_{\rm eff}=-S_{\rm eff}$, so inertia counts compare by $(n_+,n_-)\to(n_-,n_+)$ (conventions C5). This equals $\mathrm{Hess}\,S^{(4)}_{\rm eff}$ exactly at $S_2$, and $\mathrm{Hess}\,S^{(5)}_{\rm eff}+\tfrac{g^4}{600}\mathrm{Hess}(vV_4^{-1}v)+O(g^5)$ at $S_3$. At $S_4$, on the perturbative sheet away from the fold, it equals $\mathrm{Hess}\,S^{(6)}_{\rm eff}+O(g^5)$ (untruncated, or truncated with $W_6^{(\mathcal S)}$); with the full-$\mathcal C$ vertex in a truncation there is an additional $-\tfrac{g^4}{600}\mathrm{Hess}(w^\top M^{-1}w)$. These matchings are formal in $g$. The classical auxiliary carries the direct channel ($\tfrac13$ of the quartic contribution, $\tfrac15$ of the quintic one at $S_3$), and $\delta\Sigma$ exchange carries the rest. Off the saddle ($S_2,S_3$), with $\Delta=\Sigma_0-\Sigma_*(\Psi_0)$ and $H_{\Sigma\Sigma}'=\partial_\Psi H_{\Sigma\Sigma}$: $H_{\rm eff}-\mathrm{Hess}\,V_{\rm eff}=-\langle\partial_\Sigma S,\Sigma_*''\rangle-(H_{\Sigma\Sigma}'\Delta)^\top H_{\Sigma\Sigma}^{-1}(H_{\Sigma\Sigma}'\Delta)$. Scope: finite model. At the actual vertices the identities hold where $\mathcal K$ is invertible, and the on-shell matchings are Conditional(PH1, PH5) through R1, R17 and R19 (PH14 enters R19 only through its exclusion and is not used). Status: **Proven – Verified** (`codes/verify-mean-field-fluctuations.py` part 1, exact rationals). (1.15) is proved, not machine-checked. Algebraic layer; the finite-model identities need no hypothesis, the on-shell matchings at the vertices are Conditional(PH1, PH5) as stated above.

### R66 — Mean field I: the classical auxiliary is the direct channel (one third of the $\ell_3$ part of $Q_{\Psi_0}$); the $\Psi$ block is nilpotent iff $\{Q,\ell_3(\Psi_0,\Psi_0,\cdot)\}=0$ [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/060-mean-field-fluctuations.md` claim tree C2–C2.4, §2
- **Scope**: the factor $\tfrac13$, (2.5) and the Jacobiator equivalence are algebraic identities in any cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra (normalisation: conventions C4); the exchange recovery at the vertices Conditional(PH1, PH5); the non-nilpotency and the kernel statement are finite-model statements, not transferred to the vertices; the joint BRST operator is constructed in R73 (finite model) and R76 (string field)
- **Parts**: the factor, (2.5), (2.6) — Proven; the non-vanishing of $\{Q,L_3(P_0)\}$ and the kernel count — Verified in a finite model; the nilpotency of $Q_{\Psi_0}$ — Cited (Zwiebach 1993 (4.117)) and confirmed in the model
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-09-30 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-060-20260930T233400.md` – REVISE light: all 19 nodes hold, every load-bearing formula re-derived; two majors of interpretation → `agent/reports/computator-060-fix-20261001T004500.md` (congruence C1.7, surjectivity theorem C3.4, structural (2.6))
  - 2026-10-01 – node check – `agent/reports/critic-060-confirm-20261001T013600.md` – all changed nodes hold (C1.7's congruence, C3.4 under the stated codimension-2 hypothesis, (2.6) exact form; row conditionality corrected) → `agent/reports/computator-060-confirm-fix-20261001T020000.md`
  - 2026-10-01 – exact/numerical check – `codes/verify-mean-field-fluctuations.py`, run 3: 51 PASS / 0 FAIL (exact rationals, the $L_\infty$ model, mpmath 40 digits; corank one enforced at $\tau_0$ and at the five base points); orchestrator re-run `agent/scratch/mean-field-a-rerun2-orchestrator-2026-10-01.log`

**Mean field I — the auxiliary as a BRST deformation (record 060, C2).** In the normalisation $V_{n+1}=\omega(\cdot,\ell_n)$, R1's $\Psi$-block at $(\Psi_0,\Sigma_0)$ is $Q+g\ell_2(\Psi_0,\cdot)+\tfrac{g^2}3L_3(\Sigma_0)$, where $L_3$ is the linear extension of $\tfrac12\ell_3(\Psi,\Psi,\cdot)$. At $\Sigma_0=P_0$ this is one third of the $\ell_3$ part of the shifted operator $Q_{\Psi_0}$ (Zwiebach 1993 (4.112)): the direct channel. At $S_3$ the block also carries $\tfrac15$ of the $\ell_4$ part. On fields, $\omega Q_{\Psi_0}$ is recovered with the $\delta\Sigma$ exchange through the matched order (C1). The arity-3 $L_\infty$ relation gives $\{Q,L_3(P_0)\}=-\tfrac12\mathrm{Jac}_{\ell_2}(\Psi_0,\Psi_0,\cdot)-\ell_3(Q\Psi_0,\Psi_0,\cdot)$. Hence at a Maurer–Cartan point the block's square is $-\tfrac23g^2\{Q,L_3(P_0)\}+O(g^3)$, and the block is nilpotent at $O(g^2)$ iff $\{Q,\ell_3(\Psi_0,\Psi_0,\cdot)\}=0$ — equivalently, iff $\ell_2$ obeys the Jacobi identity on $(\Psi_0,\Psi_0,\cdot)$ at leading order. $\ell_3\neq0$ alone does not suffice: a minimal model has $Q=0$. This equivalence holds in any cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra. The non-vanishing, and hence the non-nilpotency of the block even at the saddle, is established in the finite model only, not at the string vertices. A deformation of $Q_{\Psi_0}$ by $\Sigma_0$ alone, $Q_{\Psi_0}+g^2L_3(\Sigma_0-P_0)$, is not an operator the cubic theory produces. It is nilpotent at $O(g^2)$ modulo the EOM iff $\Sigma_0-P_0\in\ker\{Q,L_3(\cdot)\}$ (a proper subspace whenever $\{Q,L_3(P_0)\}\neq0$), and in the model not beyond. The BRST operator on $(\delta\Psi,\delta\Sigma)$ jointly (Task 22) is not constructed in 060; it is R73 (derivation 067, Task 22a), with the string-field statement R76. Checked in a transferred matrix Chern–Simons model (058's construction with $A=\Lambda[\theta_1,\theta_2,\theta_3]$; 058's literal model has $\ell_3\equiv0$ on classical fields). Scope: the factor $\tfrac13$, (2.5) and the Jacobiator equivalence are algebraic identities. The exchange recovery at the vertices is Conditional(PH1, PH5). The non-nilpotency and the kernel are finite-model statements and are not transferred. Status: **Proven** (factor, (2.5), (2.6)) **– Verified in a finite model** (part 2). The nilpotency of $Q_{\Psi_0}$ is cited (Zwiebach (4.117)) and confirmed in the model. The all-degree operator statement with the auxiliary's BV sector is not derived in 060; it is R73 (finite model, $k\in\{2,3\}$: $H(\mathcal Q_0)\cong H(Q^{(k)}_{\Psi_0})$ in all ghost numbers to the truncation order) and R76 (string field). The $L_\infty$ normalisation is conventions C4 (`knowledge/conventions.md`).

**Mean field I — the degenerate branch (record 060, C3).** Excl. within the finite model at $S_3$: generic real symmetric tensors, $\mathcal S=\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal U$, corank one. The $\Sigma$-EOM $K_\Psi\Sigma=u(\Psi)$ has a line of solutions exactly over $\mathcal B=\{\det K_\Psi=0,\ n\cdot u=0\}$, the base points of $\Sigma_*=K^{-1}u$. $\mathcal B$ has expected codimension 2 (shown for $\dim\mathcal U=2$; a five-field instance has five real base points). The branch has dimension $\dim\mathcal U-1$, sits at $\Psi_0=O(1/g)$, and carries $S_3=A+\tfrac{g^2}{24}u^\top K^+u$, constant on each line. It is the exceptional line of the graph closure of $\Sigma_*$ over each base point, and it solves the $\Sigma$-EOM only. Theorem (*Overdetermination*): for fixed $(V_3,V_4,V_5)$ with $\mathcal B$ of codimension 2 and corank one, the set of $V_2$ admitting a joint critical point on the branch lies in a proper algebraic subset of $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal U^*$ (the wall). The reason is that $V_2\mapsto V_2\Psi_0$ is onto $\mathcal U$ and $\Psi_0\neq0$ on $\mathcal B$. In the instance the wall has codimension one; the joint critical point on it is nondegenerate and continues off the wall to a regular critical point with $\det K\neq0$. Outside the class: selection rules forcing $n\cdot u\equiv0$ on $\{\det K=0\}$ (real vertices); $S_4$ and its fold; corank $\ge2$; $V_2$ fixed by the spectrum (string case). Scope: finite model. At the vertices the structural statements (3.1)–(3.3) are Conditional(PH1, PH5); the exclusion is not transferred, since it uses genericity of $V_2$, $V_4$ and $V_5$, which the vertices do not provide (not testable with the level-(1,1) data of record 063 (Task 14b, R72), which stops at $O(g_s^2)$, where the auxiliary block is the constant invertible $A$; the test needs $V_5(T^{5-k}D^k)$, R64 (ii)). Status: **Proven – Verified** (part 3, 40 digits; the theorem is proved in the text, and the instance is machine-checked), excl. within the stated class.

**Integrating out $\Psi$ at tree level (derivation 065).** The $\Psi$-equation of R1's action is $Q\Psi+\tfrac g2\ell_2(\Psi,\Psi)+\tfrac{g^2}6\ell_3(\Sigma,\Psi)=0$ ($c_4=\tfrac16$; 060 C2.1's $\tfrac13$ is the same coefficient relative to $\tfrac{g^2}2\ell_3(\Psi_*^2,\cdot)$); $\Psi=0$ solves it for every $\Sigma$, so at $S_2$ the elimination around $\Psi=0$ is trivial, $\Gamma_0=-\tfrac{g^2}{24}V_4(\Sigma,\Sigma)$. The $\Psi$-Hessian at $\Psi=0$, $Q_\Sigma=Q+\tfrac{g^2}6\ell_3(\Sigma,\cdot)$, is $\omega$-symmetric, not nilpotent for generic $\Sigma$ ($Q_\Sigma^2=-\tfrac{g^2}6[\ell_3(Q^{(2)}\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathrm{Jac}_\Sigma]+\tfrac{g^4}{36}\ell_3(\Sigma,\ell_3(\Sigma,\cdot))$), and has a kernel exactly on the hypersurface $D=\{\det Q_\Sigma=0\}$, at $\Sigma=O(g^{-2})$. A nontrivial branch of solutions through $(0,\Sigma)$ exists iff $\Sigma\in D$ (only if: IFT; if: corank one, $V_3(\chi^3)\ne0$, transcritical, $\Psi_*=-\tfrac g3\tfrac{V_4(\delta\Sigma,\chi,\chi)}{V_3(\chi^3)}\chi+O(\delta\Sigma^2)$). On any $S_2$ branch $\Gamma_0=-\tfrac{g^2}{24}V_4(\Sigma,\Sigma)-\tfrac g{12}V_3(\Psi_*^3)$ exactly; $d\Gamma_0=\partial_\Sigma S\vert_{\Psi_*}$, so its stationary points are the joint critical points $(\Psi_c,\Psi_c^{\otimes2})$ of R1. At $S_3$ the R17 term is a $\Psi$-tadpole and $\Gamma_0=-\tfrac{g^2}{24}V_4(\Sigma^2)-\tfrac{g^6}{28800}j_\Sigma Q_\Sigma^{-1}j_\Sigma+O(g^{10})$, $j_\Sigma=V_5(\Sigma,\Sigma,\cdot)$.

**PH2 (a), the nested kernels of the dressed tower (derivation 061).** Machine-checked in the finite model (`codes/verify-ph2a.py`, run 2): C1.1, C1.2, C1.3 (descent at the top level, and one level below with the upward clause D2), C1.4 ((3.5): both clauses with break legs, the $O(g^2)$ order of the $T_6$ lift, the $K=2$ limit), C1.7 (one-dimensional excess, pole order $\le2$), C1.9, C1.10 ($k=3$). Not machine-checked: pole orders 3, 4 (4.5′); (4.9) for $k\ge4$; multi-dimensional excess kernels; about 20 of the 57 checks hold by construction or are implied by their break legs (§6); the rationality of the $n=6$ term is checked at one $\Psi$ per seed (proved in §4(f)).

In a finite-dimensional model of R26's algebraic layer (shared-parent class, tower truncated at $K$, quotient prescription), the nested kernels depend on $g\Psi$ only. Take the level-$k$ quotient by a subspace annihilated by every Jacobi block in which $\Sigma_k$ appears (descent; the joint kernel $N_{k,\infty}$, 034 (C$_\infty$) after transposing $L_k$). Then the nested conditions hold at $g=0$ iff $\ker\tilde M_k=N_{k,\infty}$ for $k=2,\dots,K$, which forces the canonical quotient. At $k=2$ the radical clause is automatic, and the content is $\ker V_4\subseteq N_{2,\infty}$: $V_5(\ker V_4,\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0,\Psi)=0$ (R38) and, for $K\ge3$, $T_6(\cdot_3;\ker V_4,\Psi)=0$, a condition on the $(3|2,1)$ block of the chosen presentation, satisfiable only if the forced diagonal vanishes on $\operatorname{Sym}^6\mathcal H_0\cap(\mathcal S_3\otimes\ker V_4\otimes\mathcal H_0)$ (non-zero e.g. when $\ker V_4\ni\chi\otimes\chi$, where it requires $V_6(\chi^6)=0$; implied by 034's (C$^{(6)}$) with R38); at $K=2$ this is R38 exactly. The conditions then hold for $\lvert g\Psi\rvert<r_{L,K}$ (any norm; the argument gives no bound uniform in $L$ or $K$), and R26's formal-series statement uses only the $g=0$ form; $[g^0]\hat H_k=\tilde M_k$ at every level. The required stability is descent: stability in $g$ together with $L_k^\top N_k=0$ (necessary; automatic at $k=2$ and for block-symmetric parents); the upward clause is necessary when the kinetic of the level above is definite on its quotient, generically otherwise. PH1 does not imply it. PH1 does not imply PH2 (b) across the class (the former PH2 strictly extends PH1, 061 C1.8): a singular $\tilde M_3$ with $V_4$ non-degenerate is admissible, and with a simple pole it is invisible in $\hat H_2(0)$ along each ray (a statement on logical strength within the class, not a claim that PH2 (b) fails for the string vertices). Such a failure is generically visible in the value as a term rational in $\Psi$: for a block-symmetric parent at $n=6$ (pole order 2) or $n=7$ (simple pole), otherwise already at $n=4$ or $n=5$. For the string vertices, where a fixed level still carries continuous momenta, the determinant and neighbourhood statements are not covered; PH1 is read as the ledger reads it.

**Mean field I at level (1,1) (derivation 063).** In the cubic HS theory at level (1,1), zero momentum, $O(g_s^2)$ (record 063; data and conventions of R68), the joint Hessian of R1's action on $(\delta t,\delta d,\delta\Sigma)$ has three kinds of block (action side, $\mathrm{Hess}\,S$; negate for the potential side $\mathcal V=-S$, conv. C5): $\Sigma\Sigma$: $-\tfrac{g_s^2}{12}A$, the Hessian of R68's block, constant; mixed: $\tfrac{g_s^2}6V_4(\delta\Sigma,\Psi_0,\delta\Psi)$, a $3\times2$ Hankel matrix linear in the background; $\Psi\Psi$: $V_2+g_sV_3(\Psi_0)+\tfrac{g_s^2}6V_4(\Sigma_0)$. At the perturbative vacuum and at the six regular critical points of record 059 ($\beta=\tfrac32,2$), R65's congruence holds. The Schur complement equals the directly computed $\mathrm{Hess}\,\mathcal V_{\rm eff}$ to $\le10^{-15}$, an exact identity here checked as a transcription test. The inertia of $\mathrm{Hess}(-S)$ is $(3,2,0)=\mathrm{In}(A)+\mathrm{In}\,\mathrm{Hess}\,\mathcal V_{\rm eff}=(2,1,0)+(1,1,0)$, and that of $\mathrm{Hess}\,S$ is $(2,3,0)$. At the origin both are $(2,2,1)$, with the dilaton zero mode shared. There are no zero modes at the critical points. The auxiliary's fluctuation directions are the subspace $\delta\Psi=0$. It is $H$-orthogonal to the shell tangent $(\delta\Psi,\Sigma_*'\delta\Psi)$, on which $H$ is $\mathrm{Hess}\,\mathcal V_{\rm eff}$ (a consequence of the congruence, exact off shell). On the potential side the restriction to $\delta\Psi=0$ is $\tfrac{g_s^2}{12}A$ at every background, with exactly one negative direction. The $TD$ coordinate direction is negative ($A_{22}=h_2<0$); in the Euclidean coordinate metric the negative eigenvector has overlap $0.988$, $0.999$ with $TD$ and Rayleigh quotient $\lambda_-(A)/12$. Freezing $\Sigma$ at its classical value keeps only the direct channel, one third of the quartic term. At the delivered data this frozen block has inertia $(0,2)$ instead of $(1,1)$ on the potential side at three of the six points: the two deep points and the $\beta=2$ $t,d>0$ point. Over R68's $h_2$ window, with $h_1,h_3$ as delivered and each point followed from the lower end of the window, the miscount persists at the two deep points up to their escape, and at the $\beta=2$ $t,d>0$ point over a window around the delivered $h_2$ (record 063 §3), below which it flips to the right count. The four $t,d>0$ and deep points run to infinity at the real double roots of $V_4((T+uD)^4)$, above the delivered $h_2$. This is a simple pole of the branch: beyond it the point returns from the opposite direction as a maximum of $\mathcal V_{\rm eff}$, with joint inertia $(2,3,0)$ and frozen count $(1,1)$ against $(0,2)$; the returned $t,d>0$ branch annihilates with the valley point at the valley's fold, and the returned deep branch persists up to $h_2\to0$. On the tracked branches, from the lower end of the window to the pole or fold, the joint inertia is $(3,2,0)$. The $\delta\Sigma$ exchange restores the count, so a fluctuation analysis around a mean-field saddle needs the exchange (the mean-field versus RPA distinction; freezing $\Sigma-\Psi^{\otimes2}$ instead of $\Sigma$ gives the right count, since in the variables $(\Psi,\Sigma'=\Sigma-\Psi^{\otimes2})$ the action splits exactly, $S=S_{\rm eff}(\Psi)-\tfrac{g_s^2}{24}V_4(\Sigma',\Sigma')$ at $O(g_s^2)$). Euclidean eigenvectors mix $\Psi$ and $\Sigma$ at order one; they depend on the basis and on $g_s$ and are recorded in 063 §3 only. Status: **Verified** (120/120, numerical, double precision on the delivered data; the $h_2$ pattern by regression assertion), no PH.

**Mean field II (record 069).** At $S_2$ with weight $e^{S/\hbar}$ (C5), the exact $\Sigma$-equation of the quantum theory is $V_4(\Sigma-\langle\Psi\otimes\Psi\rangle_\Sigma,\cdot)=0$: the auxiliary is the pair correlator (formal identity; at $S_3$ the R17 term adds $\tfrac{g^3}{60}V_5(\Sigma,\langle\Psi\rangle_\Sigma,\cdot)$). Its Gaussian evaluation ($V_3$ dropped) is $\mathcal V_{\rm eff}[\Sigma]=\tfrac{g^2}{24}V_4(\Sigma,\Sigma)+\tfrac\hbar2\operatorname{Tr}\log(-\omega Q_\Sigma)$, $Q_\Sigma=Q+\tfrac{g^2}6\ell_3(\Sigma,\cdot)$ (R69), with gap equation $\Sigma=\hbar G_\Sigma$ on $\mathrm{Sym}^2/\ker V_4$, $G_\Sigma=(-\omega Q_\Sigma)^{-1}$: the mean field is the self-consistent $\Sigma$-dressed propagator. Its dressing $\tfrac{\hbar g^2}6\ell_3(G,\cdot)$ is one third of the Hartree (leading 2PI) self-energy $\tfrac{\hbar g^2}2\ell_3(G,\cdot)$ of the quartic theory — the direct channel of R66; the other two thirds are the $\Sigma$-exchange diagrams that a mean field drops. $\hbar\to0$ gives R69's $\Gamma_0$; at $\Psi_0=0$ the potential equals R65's one-loop joint potential up to the constant $\Sigma$-block term; loop parameter $\hbar g^2$; perturbative root $\Sigma_*=\hbar G+\tfrac{\hbar^2g^2}6GV_4(G)G+O(\hbar^3)$; in the finite model a further root sits at distance $O(\hbar g^2)$ (in $g^2\Sigma$) from each wall of $D$, a maximum of $\mathcal V_{\rm eff}$ along the normal, $\mathcal V_{\rm eff}\to-\infty$ on $D$. Obstacles to a BCS analogue (Argued): no density-of-states enhancement — the massless point is measure zero in the loop momentum and the tachyon shell shifts the pole without a Nambu structure ($\Psi$ real: no anomalous channel), the PV shell integral analytic in the shift; no Fermi-surface power counting and no $1/N$ to justify dropping internal $\Sigma$ lines; $V_3$ makes the slice $\Psi_0=0$ non-stationary at $O(\hbar g)$; at level (1,1), zero momentum, without loop momentum, $\det Q_\sigma$ vanishes linearly at $\sigma=0$ ($V_2(D,D)=0$), a stationary point at $g^2\sigma\sim\sqrt{\hbar g^2}$ along $A$-negative rays only, ray-restricted: it is not a root of the full gap equation (1.6), whose two roots at that scale are a complex pair (annotation 2026-10-01, record 070 (3.4)); the resummed object is one third of one region ($\mathcal V_{0,4}$ with two legs sewn) of $\mathcal M_{1,2}$, gauge-variant. Status: Proven – Verified for the transcription in the finite model; the obstacles are Argued.

**Annotation (2026-10-01, after record 070).** Record 070 (3.4): the two roots of the level-(1,1) gap equation at the scale $\hat\sigma\sim\sqrt{\hbar g^2}$ are the complex pair $\det\mathcal Q=\pm i\sqrt{\lambda h_4/6}\,(1+O(\sqrt\lambda))$ (both $\beta$, $h_4>0$); no real root of the three-dimensional equation tends to the origin. (2.5a) remains correct as a ray-restricted stationarity along $A$-negative rays, but the stationary point does not lift to a root of (1.6): the 'dilaton zero-mode root' of O5 does not exist at level (1,1). R74's (2.5a) is ray-restricted.

**The multiplier remedy on $\ker V_4$ survives at $O(g_s^4)$ (derivation 068).** With the multiplier pair $g_s^2\langle\lambda,\Pi_N^{(C)}(\Sigma-\Psi^{\otimes2})\rangle$ on $N=\ker V_4$ (034 (3.1); $C$ a complement of $N$, $\Pi_N^{(C)}$ the projector on $N$ along $C$) and the dressed vertex $W_6^{(C)}=V_6-\tfrac65\mathcal C^{(C)}$, $\mathcal C^{(C)}$ being R19's $\Sigma$-channel composite with $V_4^{-1}$ replaced by $G_C=B(B^{\rm T}V_4B)^{-1}B^{\rm T}$, the symmetric reflexive generalised inverse of $V_4$ with image $C$ ($V_4G_CV_4=V_4$, $G_CV_4G_C=G_C$, $G_CV_4=\pi_C$), the eliminated action equals $S^{(6)}_{\rm eff}+O(g_s^5)$ as an identity in $\Psi$ for every complement; the $O(g_s^5)$ residual is 013's $A_5$ with the same replacement. Neither R38's (1.2) nor 034's (C$^{(6)}$) is needed: $\Sigma$ stays on $\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0$ and nothing descends. The vertex is complement-dependent iff $V_5(\ker V_4,\Psi^{\otimes2},\Psi)\not\equiv0$ (R38's (1.2) for totally symmetric $V_5$): $\langle v,G_{C_L}v\rangle$ is a non-constant quadratic in $L\in\operatorname{Hom}(C_0,\ker V_4)$ (068 (2.2)), and no complement-independent $W_6$ serves two complements *with the constraint undeformed and $V_5(\Sigma,\Sigma,\Psi)$ the only $O(g_s^3)$ coupling*; an $O(g_s^3)$ composite coupling $g_s^3\langle\lambda,Z_C(\Sigma,\Psi)\rangle$ can carry the complement dependence instead (068 (2.5)–(2.6); check C3.6-relax, corank 1 and 2), so the presentation datum is (constraint, $O(g_s^3)$ constraint coupling, vertex) and the eliminated action is complement-independent through $O(g_s^4)$. Under R38 the composite tensor is complement-independent and R19 is recovered; the Moore–Penrose inverse is one member of the family with no distinguished role (PH1: no auxiliary inner product). String transfer: Conditional(PH1, PH14 [$O(g_s^4)$, for the exclusion legs]) plus R13's regularity of $\ker V_4$ (closed, complemented, graded complement); applies in the branch where PH5's second clause fails. Not covered: the hybrid's gauge law and BV pairing (R13's open item restricted to $N$), the $O(g_s^5)$ closure and beyond.

Second proof of R73 C3 by the linearisation lemma (6.3) in R60's chart. $k=4$ (with $\Sigma_3$): the same with two pairs (6.6), coefficients $\mathcal K^{(4)}_{\Psi_0}$ and $\tilde M$, $O(g^5)$, under R62's $k=4$ hypotheses (incl. its source-compatibility clauses and R38 when $\ker V_4\ne0$), $\mathcal K^{(4)}_{\Psi_0}$ invertible on $\mathcal S$ and $\tilde M$ on $\mathrm{Sym}^3\mathcal H_0/\ker\tilde M$ in the same sense – Proven, not machine-checked. Level truncation: (i), (iii) exact for the truncated theory as an algebraic system (R65; at $k=3$ with $\ker V_4\vert_L=0$ or R38 on $\mathcal S_L$), nilpotency fails from $g^2$ for both theories – a statement about the classical fluctuation spectrum, not cohomology. $O(1/g)$ backgrounds: pointwise similarity with the invertibility of $\mathcal K_{\Psi_0}$ nonperturbative; $T^{\rm eff}$ is exactly the operator of $S^{(k+2)}_{\rm eff}+\mathbb F_k$, $\mathbb F_2=0$, $\mathbb F_3=\tfrac{g^4}{600}vK_\Psi^{-1}v$, $\mathbb F^{\rm dr}_4$ the exact remainder of 058 (7.6), with Hessian of the same nominal size as the first neglected vertex.

### The three HS-section corrections (verbatim from the record)

1. **l. 252** — the second measure is written $\frac{\dd^dk_1}{(2\pi)^d}$ twice; it should read $\frac{\dd^d k_1}{(2\pi)^d}\frac{\dd^d k_2}{(2\pi)^d}$ (typo).
2. **l. 256** — "symmetric in the internal indices" should read "symmetric under the joint exchange $(\alpha_1,k_1)\leftrightarrow(\alpha_2,k_2)$" (eq. (1.2); all strands have $N_{\rm gh}=2$, so the exchange sign is $+1$ and "graded" is needed only if the two-string basis includes odd strands): the exchange symmetry of $\operatorname{Sym}^2\mathcal H$ acts on the whole strand label, and symmetry in $\alpha$ at fixed $(k_1,k_2)$ is a different object.
3. **l. 257–258** — "translation invariance allows reducing the momentum dependence to $\sigma_{\alpha_1\alpha_2}(k)$" holds for translation-invariant configurations (a saddle, an expectation value; $\sigma\propto\delta^d(k_1+k_2)\tilde\sigma(\ell)$, eq. (3.1)) and not for a general fluctuation, which depends on the total momentum $P=k_1+k_2$ and the relative momentum $\ell$; translation invariance makes the quadratic action diagonal in $P$ (eq. (2.2)) with an integral kernel in $\ell$ — this is the precise form of "the kinetic term will not be local": in the (Euclidean) relative coordinate an integral operator (at level 0 a smoothing one, eq. (2.5)), and in the total momentum an entire, non-polynomial function of $P^2$, i.e. an infinite-derivative operator in the centre-of-mass coordinate (eq. (4.2)). The sentence on $(n-1)$-locality: "a translation-invariant configuration of the $n$-string auxiliary field depends on $n-1$ relative momenta; a general fluctuation depends on $n$ momenta, one total and $n-1$ relative, and its quadratic action is diagonal in the total one" (eq. (3.3)). The paper's definition is the general one (Harold's decision of 2026-10-01).

- **Origin**: the question of 2026-10-04 (the $n$-puncture patch in the atlas / transition-function picture of SFT); the discussion paragraph is record §6.1; the $n\ge3$ reading (patch = asymmetric vertex region $\mathcal V'_{n+1}$ with its own section of local coordinates; the $4k-6$ $c$'s of $p_{k,k}^{-1}$ read as the circle's CKV pair plus the duals of the patches' internal antighosts) is the record's proposal

**The $n$-patch in the atlas picture (record 077).** A genus-zero Feynman region is $N$ puncture discs, $N-2$ rigid pants and $N-3$ transition functions $w_aw_b=q$ carrying the moduli and the antighosts; at genus zero the pants are dispensable as charts (global coordinate), not as vertices, and the physical atlas data are the $N$ local coordinates and the clustering (C1). An $n$-patch is an $(n+1)$-punctured sphere whose special coordinate is $1/u$ for a global $u$ (determined by its 2-jet); two patches glued by one transition function carry $2n+1$ parameters for $2n-3$ moduli, the redundancy being $\mathrm{PSL}(2,\mathbb C)\times\mathbb C^*_\kappa$ — the Möbius freedom of the global coordinate (whose dilation subgroup, with $q$ fixed, is the seam: it moves the collar circle, the two circle states change by $\lambda^{\pm L_0}$, the pairing is invariant) and the rescaling of one special coordinate compensated in $q$; rigid patches fix $G$ by convention, at $n=2$ 074's $s$-circle (C2). Two rigid patches give one complex modulus, equal to $2n-3$ iff $n=2$; for $n\ge3$ the count closes iff each patch carries $d_n=n-2$ internal moduli, $\dim\mathcal M_{0,n+1}$ — the $n$-patch is then the standard $(n+1)$-vertex chart with one distinguished leg, an asymmetric vertex region with its own section of local coordinates, and the transition function's region must be fibred over the internal moduli so that the three cover $\mathcal V_{0,2n}$ once (the analogue of 074's $\sigma_V(\theta)$; proven at $n=2$, a condition at $n\ge3$); general tree identity $\sum_kV_k(k-3)+E_{\rm int}=N-3$; trinion couplings $n-1$ moduli per patch leg, $a+b+c-3$ in all (C3). The dressed patch state has ghost number $2n-2(n-2)=4$ for every $n\ge2$, two of them glued by the $b_0^+b_0^-$-dressed plumbing give 6, second route $\hat d=4n-6=2(n-2)+2(n-2)+2$; the HS transition function is the standard plumbing on the patch states with a vertex-region range, non-level-matched transported states (twist sector by sector) and a propagator that is not a gluing (R80); the generalised tree has gluing edges ($+1$ complex modulus, $-2$ ghost number) and $\Sigma_k$ contraction edges ($-(2k-3)$, $+(4k-6)$), forced by the composite count, $k=2$ being 074 C3/C4 (C4). $n=1$ is the standard leg; $n=2$ is 071 (A.1)–(A.3), (A.9) and 074 (1.1)–(1.4); HS trees at $N=4,6$ all have $N-3$ moduli, so counting cannot distinguish the two expansions — the difference lies in the regions and the local coordinates (C5).

### R83 — Fold-arc convergence of the sector expansion of the fixed-halves gluing (tachyon inputs, $\beta=3/2$): the region transforms and per-sector statements of R80 are unaffected; the sector decomposition of $p^{\rm circ}$'s matrix elements converges in every order iff the cross-channel exponent $\alpha>-1$, in level order for $\alpha>-3$, $h_0<3$, and is Abel-summable always [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/079-fold-arc-convergence.md` C1–C4; `codes/verify-fold-arc-convergence.py` 14/14 PASS, seed 20261004 (`agent/scratch/computator/computator-079-fix-20261004T105800/run-fix.log`; run 1: 13/13, before the fix pass).
- **Scope**: fixed-halves model (074 A5, a model assumption); four tachyon inputs with arbitrary momenta subject to conservation; $\beta=3/2$; real momenta (complex: $\Re a_\pm$, not run); excited inputs: see the 2026-10-04 verification line (record 084). For the actual $p_{2,2}$: on shell at four points its integrand is the same function of $q$ (074 C1.4, modulo 032 (4.5)), so the on-shell regimes (4.1) and the Abel statement hold for $p_{2,2}$ itself on four on-shell tachyons; off shell nothing transfers (the dressed gluing is not a function of $L_0^\pm$, 074 (1.6)).
- **Statement**: the integrand is $\mathcal N|y|^{h_0}|1-y|^{2a_-}|1+y|^{2a_+}$, $y=q/4\beta^2$, $a_-+a_+=-h_0$. The sector coefficients satisfy $c_{h,m}(4\beta^2)^h=\mathcal Ng_Ng_{\bar N}\sim h^{-2\alpha-2}$ at fixed $m$, and the level-$h$ term is $\sim h^{-\alpha-1}$ (oscillating) at fixed $\theta$ on the arcs and $h^{h_0-1}$ at the arc midpoint. Here $\alpha=\min a_\pm$ is the exponent of the cross-channel collisions at $\theta=0,\pi$ on the fold circle, outside $\bar Q_V$, not of anything on the arcs. $\sum_{h,m}c_{h,m}\hat f_m(h)$ is absolutely convergent iff $\alpha>-1$ (on shell: $\alpha't,\alpha'u<-4$, the cross-channel tachyon poles). For $\alpha\le-1$ every fixed-$m$ sector sum with $A_m\ne0$ diverges like $H^{-2\alpha-2}$ ($\log H$ at $\alpha=-1$). The level-ordered sum converges to the region integral for $\alpha>-3$ and $h_0<3$ (absolutely for $\alpha>-2$, $h_0<2$). For every momentum the region integral is the Abel sum of the level series. The convergence is polynomial on the fold arcs and geometric off them (ratio $\tfrac14$ at $\theta=\pi$). R80's closed forms, $1/\hat f_m$, the real zeros of $\hat f_{m\ne0}$ and C3's per-sector identity are statements about the region transform and single eigenspaces, and are unaffected. The pole reading of R80 (C2.4's pole clause and C4, under PH31) is a statement about the level-order or Abel remainder, not about sector-by-sector sums: in fixed-$m$ sector order the remainder is not defined for $\alpha\le-1$ (four-point form; the five-point composite is not computed).
- **Status**: C1 (closed form, Darboux growth) — Verified (exact on-shell match with an independent Möbius frame; Darboux against exact coefficients to $N=1600$). C2.1–C2.2 (bound, absolute threshold $\alpha>-1$ in both directions) — Proven modulo the sampled piecewise monotonicity of $\sigma_V$ and the Numerical arc geometry of R80 C1.3 ($\cos\theta_1=\tfrac7{25}$, $\sigma_V(0)=\ln\tfrac94$, $\beta=3/2$); C2.3–C2.4 (level-order thresholds, Abel sum) — Argued (leading-order endpoint/coalescence analysis (2.4)), Numerical at six momenta; C2.5 — Numerical. C3 — Proven (a reading of 074's statements against C2). C4.1 — Verified (sympy). C4.3 (excited inputs) — shift Verified, thresholds per clause as the 2026-10-04 line (record 084).
- **Verification**:
  - 2026-10-04 – excited inputs (record 084, `codes/verify-fold-arc-excited.py` 12/12 PASS, seed 20261004, orchestrator re-run identical; critic `agent/reports/critic-084-20261004T125000.md` REVISE light → fix, `critic-084-iter2-20261004T134000.md` REVISE light (wording) → fix 2, orchestrator self-confirmation, logged choice): C4.3's shift Verified — matter-oscillator inputs of total holomorphic level $N_{\rm tot}$, generic polarisations: $\alpha\to\alpha-N_{\rm tot}$ at the endpoints and $h_0\to h_0+2N_{\rm tot}$ at the arc midpoint (the latter missed by 079); for any polarisations these thresholds are sufficient (Argued). On shell the shift cancels against the masses: every order **if** $\alpha't,\alpha'u<-4$ and level order **if** $\alpha't,\alpha'u<0$, $\alpha'(t+u)<-6$, for all on-shell matter-oscillator inputs; **iff for generic polarisations** (every order: both colliding pairs of each cross channel couple to the channel tachyon; level order: moreover a non-zero arc-midpoint coefficient). Outside that class the thresholds follow a per-chirality rule (argued); e.g. for $B$-fields with tachyons the $u$-channel modulus exponent softens to $2a_+-2$ (checked), the every-order region not derived. This entry's $\alpha's>-10$ ($>-8$) is the tachyon case of $\alpha's>-10+4N_{\rm tot}$ ($-8+4N_{\rm tot}$). Off shell the regimes are level-dependent; Abel summability for all matter-oscillator inputs (ghost-oscillator inputs not covered); the transfer to the actual $p_{2,2}$ stays stated for four on-shell tachyons. 084's unbalanced-polarisation criterion was withdrawn after its critic (the cross Darboux products dominate: counterexample machine-checked).
  - 2026-10-04 – critic review – `agent/reports/critic-079-20261004T102600.md` – every equality re-derived by hand and holds (positions, closed form (1.4), $a_-+a_++h_0=0$, arcs strictly between the collisions, Darboux, bound, thresholds, Abel, on-shell map); REVISE, light (1 major: the entry omitted C3.3 and (3.5)(ii); 7 minor) → fix pass `agent/reports/computator-079-fix-20261004T105800.md`, orchestrator self-confirmation (the critic asked for no further pass; the new check `C2.3-h0-threshold` is the one the critic specified, run by the orchestrator)
  - 2026-10-04 – exact/numerical check – `codes/verify-fold-arc-convergence.py`, 14/14 PASS: `C1.1-onshell` (break: no ghost factor), `C1.1-ghost`, `C1.2-coeffs`, `C1.3-darboux-m` (break: exponent $-\alpha-1$), `C1.3-darboux-theta` (break: generic exponent at $\pi/2$), `C2.1-profile`, `C3.1-table` (reproduces 074's zero of $\hat f_4$), `C2.2-absolute`, `C2.2-sector-divergence` (break: stationary for $\alpha>-1$), `C2.3-level-order` (exponents at C and E to $0.1$, subdominant rejected; break: D diverges), `C2.3-h0-threshold` (F diverges through the midpoint), `C2.4-abel` (break: wrong $\hat f_0$), `C2.5-rays` (break: fold-arc ray not exponential), `C4.1-onshell-map` (break: no conservation); orchestrator re-run identical
- **Consequence for R80**: 074 (1.5)'s "with the $1/h$ of the $s$-integral as the only damping" should read: the integrated form gains a further $1/|m|$ (sector order) or $1/n$ (level order) from the arcs' angular width, and convergence is set by the cross-channel exponent $\alpha$. 074 (1.5)'s "for finite-level inputs … no convergence hypothesis enters" holds for the region integral (and the open disc), not for the level-sum form on $\bar Q_V$, which touches $|y|=1$: there the threshold $\alpha>-1$ applies to every-order summation, and excited inputs lower $\alpha$ ((3.5)(ii), (4.3)). The pole reading under PH31 (R80 C2.4's pole clause, C4) needs the level-order or Abel remainder (C3.3). The §6.3 item "not checked: fold arcs" is closed by this entry.

- **Origin**: PH31's registered falsifier (discussion of 2026-10-01 §4, action 3); the mechanism (an extra conformal Killing vector of the $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$ vertex, whose conservation law intertwines $b_0^-,L_0^-$ on the inputs with $O_b,O_L$ on the output) is the record's finding

**The selection rule (record 078).** At $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$ the ordinary local maps of $\mathcal V_3'$ are pure dilations, $f_0'(w)=w/\gamma'$, and the dilation of the $z$-sphere fixing the two ordinary punctures acts as $+w\partial_w$ in one ordinary frame and $-w\partial_w$ in the other; the conservation law of its antighost and stress-tensor contours gives $(b_1^--z^2b_{-1}^-)\Phi_2(\Sigma)=0$ for $\Sigma$ with $b_0^-$-annihilated legs and $(L_1^--z^2L_{-1}^-)\Phi_2(\Sigma)=0$ for level-matched legs, for inputs of every level. The image of $\mathrm{Sym}^2\mathcal H_0$ under the level-projected circle map therefore misses a proper subspace of the even block $W^{\rm even}$ at every output level $K\ge2$ (5 of 73 at $K=2$; at $K=3$, 60 of 487 for $d_{\rm eff}=2$ and 107 of 1447 for $d_{\rm eff}=3$), however large the input level; PH31 is refuted for that vertex, for the extended map too. For $\alpha'_{\rm v}\ne0$ the vector field picks up a $w^2\partial_w$ term in the local frames, no selection rule follows (no every-level rule; at input level $L$ there are level-limited $c$-ghost relations once $K\ge2L$, record 093), and the map is onto the block at $K\le3$ for $L\le2$ ($d_{\rm eff}=2$; also $d_{\rm eff}=3$ at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=1/2$, $K=3$). Consequences: R80's three PH31-conditional clauses ($GP_{\mathcal I}=G|_W$, the pole reading of the real zeros, the six-point density C4) lose their support in the fixed-halves model at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$ and keep PH31 as a finite-level-supported hypothesis at $\alpha'_{\rm v}\ne0$; which model the project adopts is to be decided. A weakened hypothesis PH31′ (surjectivity onto the joint kernel of the two identities, plus a statement on $x=G_W^{-1}G_5'\Phi_3^{[b]}$) is a candidate, not registered. By-product: the exchange rule of 071 C3 is machine-checked in the ghost sector.

### R85 — excl. within the fixed-halves model at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$: the six-point composite is the projected one, $\mathcal C=\mathcal C_{\rm sector}-\Delta$; the five-point vector lies in the selection-identity kernel only under a fine-tuned dressing identity, so PH31′ is no rescue of R80 C4 [mixed]

- **Source**: `agent/derivations/082-ph31prime.md` C1–C5, §1–§5; `codes/verify-ph31prime.py` 11/11 PASS, seed 20261004 (orchestrator re-run identical)
- **Scope**: see the scope paragraph below; hypotheses in force: none (A5, A5′ model assumptions)
- **Verification**:

- **Status**: exact form (4.1)–(4.2) Proven; $O_b\Phi_3^{[b]}\ne0$ Proven (witness ghost algebra); $O_bx\ne0$ for the model's dressing Argued (C3.5, gap); $\Delta\ne0$ conditional on it; the pole criterion (C4.5) open
- **Origin**: the candidate PH31′ of record 078 §5, tested on a general instruction to proceed with all open tasks (2026-10-04); the recommendation is in the record's last section

**Statement.**
In the fixed-halves model (A5 with the toy A5′) at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$, R80 C4 reads exactly $\mathcal C=\int\omega(\Phi_3,G_5\pi_WP_{\mathcal I}x)=\mathcal C_{\rm sector}-\Delta$ with $x=\pi_W\pi_{\rm even}F^{-1}F_5'\Phi_3^{[b]}$; the density $\mu_{\mathcal C}$ of R80 is that of $\mathcal C_{\rm sector}$. On the cluster ket $O_b$ represents the CKV $\beta(t^2-z^2)\partial_t$ (R84's $O_b$ via $b^-[\tilde v]=\beta O_b$), which does not vanish at generic cluster punctures: $O_b\Phi_3^{[b]}\ne0$ (three tachyons, component $c_0\bar c_0\bar c_1|P\rangle$, coefficient $\propto(t_2-t_1)(t_1t_2+z^2)$). For the dressed $x$ that component is $-\tau_{12}\int d^2t_3\,JM\,[z^2k_0(h_0;t_3)+t_1t_2k_2(h_0+2;t_3)]$, $k_m=\hat f_5/\hat f_m$; $x\in\ker O_b$ needs this to vanish for all momenta, a fine-tuned identity between $Q_5$ and $Q_V$ that nothing motivates. Argued, not shown, that the model's dressing violates it (route: momentum variation at fixed $P^2$ forces the reflection-symmetrised bracket to vanish pointwise in $t_3$; the poles of $k_0$, $k_2$ in $h_0$ must then cancel); falsifier: an explicit $Q_5$ for which the symmetrised bracket vanishes identically. Hence PH31′ cannot be registered as a motivated rescue of R80 C4. The split $\mathcal C_{\rm sector}$, $\Delta$ is exact where both sides converge (R83: $\alpha>-1$, level order $\alpha>-3$, $h_0<3$, otherwise Abel sums); $\det p$ means the level-truncated compression. Open: $O_bx\ne0$ (argued), $\Delta\ne0$ (genericity, conditional on it), where the poles of the projected composite sit – zeros of the compressed $\det p$, at the real zeros of $\hat f_4,\hat f_6$ if $\pi_W\mathcal I$ contains pure-sector vectors there (generically only if), otherwise moved, not removed.

Checks: `C2-taylor` (mode coefficients of a CKV at a puncture, sympy series, with break leg); `C2-ward-ghost` (mode algebra vs contour identity, with Koszul-sign break leg); `C3-witness-ward` (undressed witness, exact, non-zero at seeded points); `C3-witness-dressed-transcription` (transcription of the dressed coefficient, with a level-swap break leg; non-vanishing for the model's dressing not checked); `C3-locus-dressed` (at $t_1t_2=-z^2$ the dressed coefficient is $-(t_2-t_1)z^2(k_{00}-k_{20})$); `C3-limit` with `BREAK-limit` (zeros of the CKV on the punctures recover 078's identity for the undressed state) – `codes/verify-ph31prime.py`, 11/11 PASS, seed 20261004.

**Scope of the exclusion.** Class: the fixed-halves model with rigid $\mathcal V_3'$ halves at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$ and the toy A5′ on the five-point side, generic momenta and cluster moduli – the model in which R80 C4 was stated and PH31 refuted. Relevant cases outside: $\alpha'_{\rm v}\ne0$ (no selection rule on $\mathcal H_0$, PH31 not refuted, 078); the actual $p_{2,2}$ with dressed $\Sigma$ legs (no diagonal $G_5$); dressings fine-tuned to (3.4). Weakest relaxation that evades it: $\alpha'_{\rm v}\ne0$.

### R86 — The R84 selection rule is an artefact of rigid dilation coordinates: a CKV reads $\pm w\partial_w$ in a local frame iff the local map is Möbius with pole at the partner puncture ($\mu_i=0$); the conservation law carries the whole germ, and for Möbius maps it is the two-term law $\frac1\beta O_b\Phi_2(A_1,A_2)=\Phi_2\big((\mu_1b_1-\mu_1^*\bar b_1)A_1,A_2\big)-(-1)^{|A_1|}\Phi_2\big(A_1,(\mu_2b_1-\mu_2^*\bar b_1)A_2\big)$, $A_i\in\mathcal H_0$, with complex $\mu_i(q)$; for the actual $S_4$-symmetric $\mathcal V_4$, $\mu_i(q)\ne0$ on an open subset of $Q_V$, at almost every point Conditional(PH34), and at every point of $Q_V$ given the vertex section's explicit formulas (records 089, 092), for every $\beta$ in the stated range; $\mu_1=\mu_2=0$ on the single surface $Z=\tfrac12$ (fold boundary of $Q_V$), forced by symmetry, and over $\mathcal F_s\cup\{|q|=1\}$ the circle states are those of the theory's cubic vertex, the $\alpha'_{\rm v}=\tfrac12$, $\gamma'=\beta$ member, with $\mu_i=\tfrac1{2\beta}$ ($\tfrac13$ at $\beta=\tfrac32$) (C3.2 restated after PH33's refutation); where $\mu_1\ne0$, $O_b\mathcal I_q\ne0$ (symmetrised pair $A_1\odot A_2$, $A_1=c_{-1}\bar b_{-1}c_1\bar c_1|k_1\rangle=-c_{-1}c_1|k_1\rangle$, $A_2=c_1\bar c_1|k_2\rangle$, through its odd-sector component $-2\beta z^2(\mu_1+\mu_2)a_1^{-2}\bar a_1^{-1}$ on $c_1c_0\bar c_0|k_1+k_2\rangle$); the law relates the even- and odd-$L_0^-$-sector images and imposes no linear condition on $\mathcal I_q$ alone; on inputs with ghost part $c_1\bar c_1$ the $b$-rule survives for every section (the $L$-rule on matter primaries); so the refutation of PH31 is a property of the fixed-halves model at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=0$, not of $p_{2,2}$ off shell [mixed · PH33 refuted: the $|q|=1$ and $\mathcal F_s$ clauses restated at $\alpha'_{\rm v}=\tfrac12$, $\gamma'=\beta$; the a.e. clause Conditional(PH34); every point of $Q_V$ Proven given the companion package]
